# Copy-paste commands

Generated by tools/render-paste-commands.py from the repo files. Do not edit by hand.

Each block is self-contained: paste it into an SSH session on the server as the admin user (`ops`); the
blocks use `sudo`. Before the users block has run, paste as root, which works too. A block writes the files it needs to
`/root/server-init-config` and runs the installer, which checks its own result at the end. Use the blocks in the order below and skip
what you don't need. Long blocks paste best into a plain terminal or tmux; if a paste breaks off, just paste the
whole block again, the installers are safe to rerun or tell you the component is already installed.

- [Base host](#base) (only if the server was created without the init)
- [Users: admin, service accounts, SSH hardening](#users)
- [Admin tools: htop, btop, ncdu, iotop and others](#packages)
- [Health, cleanup, job isolation, engine updates](#tools)
- [Host firewall and fail2ban](#firewall)
- [Security tools: audit, network, scan](#security)
- [Register the runner](#register)
- [Per-job limits](#job-limits)
- [Docker Hub pull-through cache](#registry-cache)
- [Monitoring: Prometheus, node_exporter, Grafana](#monitoring)
- [Logging: Loki + Grafana Alloy](#logging)
- [npm cache (Verdaccio)](#npm-cache)
- [Shared cache /srv/ci-cache](#shared-cache)
- [Image prewarm](#prewarm)
- [Grafana on a domain (HTTPS via Caddy)](#grafana-domain)
- [SELinux enforcing](#selinux)
- [Lock root SSH](#lock-root)
- [Update everything installed](#update)
- [Everything at once](#all)

<a id="base"></a>
## Base host: Docker, GitLab Runner, tools

The same steps as `rocky-clean.yaml`, for a Rocky server created without it. Skip this if you used the init. Paste it as root (that is how a server without the init is reached). It does not create the `ops` admin: paste the [Users](#users) block next. Root SSH stays open until the [Lock root SSH](#lock-root) block.

````bash
sudo bash <<'INSTALL'
set -Eeuo pipefail
dnf -y -q upgrade --refresh
dnf -y -q install sudo curl ca-certificates git jq tmux dnf-automatic policycoreutils-python-utils gnupg2 bind-utils lsof sysstat tcpdump traceroute nmap-ncat

install -d "$(dirname /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/00-ci-hardening.conf)"
cat > /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/00-ci-hardening.conf <<'__SIC_EOF__'
PasswordAuthentication no
KbdInteractiveAuthentication no
PermitRootLogin prohibit-password
X11Forwarding no
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 600 /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/00-ci-hardening.conf

grep -qxF -- 'precedence ::ffff:0:0/96  100' /etc/gai.conf 2>/dev/null || echo 'precedence ::ffff:0:0/96  100' >> /etc/gai.conf

install -d "$(dirname /etc/systemd/journald.conf.d/50-size.conf)"
cat > /etc/systemd/journald.conf.d/50-size.conf <<'__SIC_EOF__'
[Journal]
SystemMaxUse=500M
MaxRetentionSec=14day
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/systemd/journald.conf.d/50-size.conf

install -d "$(dirname /etc/docker/daemon.json)"
cat > /etc/docker/daemon.json <<'__SIC_EOF__'
{
  "log-driver": "json-file",
  "log-opts": { "max-size": "50m", "max-file": "3" }
}
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/docker/daemon.json

install -d "$(dirname /etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml)"
cat > /etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml <<'__SIC_EOF__'
concurrent = 2
check_interval = 0
listen_address = "127.0.0.1:9252"
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 600 /etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml

install -d "$(dirname /root/.tmux.conf)"
cat > /root/.tmux.conf <<'__SIC_EOF__'
set -g mouse on
set -g history-limit 50000
set -g base-index 1
set -g status-right '#H  %H:%M'
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /root/.tmux.conf

install -d "$(dirname /etc/profile.d/ci-runner.sh)"
cat > /etc/profile.d/ci-runner.sh <<'__SIC_EOF__'
alias health='sudo /usr/local/bin/health'
alias runner-logs='sudo journalctl -u gitlab-runner -f'
alias runner-errors='sudo journalctl -u gitlab-runner --since "-30min" --no-pager | grep -Ei "error|warn|bottleneck"'
alias runner-jobs='sudo docker ps --filter label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true --format "{{.Names}}  {{.Status}}"'
alias runner-metrics='curl -s 127.0.0.1:9252/metrics | grep "^gitlab_runner_"'
alias dfd='df -h / ; sudo docker system df'
alias dstats='sudo docker stats --no-stream'
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/profile.d/ci-runner.sh

install -d "$(dirname /etc/yum.repos.d/docker-ce.repo)"
cat > /etc/yum.repos.d/docker-ce.repo <<'__SIC_EOF__'
[docker-ce-stable]
name=Docker CE Stable
baseurl=https://download.docker.com/linux/rhel/$releasever/$basearch/stable
enabled=1
gpgcheck=1
gpgkey=file:///etc/pki/rpm-gpg/docker-ce.asc
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/yum.repos.d/docker-ce.repo

install -d "$(dirname /etc/yum.repos.d/gitlab-runner.repo)"
cat > /etc/yum.repos.d/gitlab-runner.repo <<'__SIC_EOF__'
[runner_gitlab-runner]
name=GitLab Runner
baseurl=https://packages.gitlab.com/runner/gitlab-runner/el/$releasever/$basearch
enabled=1
gpgcheck=1
repo_gpgcheck=1
gpgkey=file:///etc/pki/rpm-gpg/gitlab-runner.asc file:///etc/pki/rpm-gpg/gitlab-runner-pkg.asc
metadata_expire=300
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/yum.repos.d/gitlab-runner.repo

install -d "$(dirname /usr/local/sbin/pin-repo-key)"
cat > /usr/local/sbin/pin-repo-key <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Download a package repository signing key and install it only if it has the expected fingerprint.
# Usage: pin-repo-key URL FINGERPRINT DEST
# The fingerprint is the 40 hex digits of the primary key (spaces allowed). The file is checked with gpg in an empty
# keyring; only the key with that fingerprint is written to DEST (anything else in the download is dropped).
# On a mismatch nothing is written and the exit code is 1, so the repository is never trusted with a swapped key.
set -Eeuo pipefail

if [[ $# -ne 3 || "$1" == "-h" || "$1" == "--help" ]]; then
  echo "Usage: $0 URL FINGERPRINT DEST" >&2
  exit 2
fi
url="$1"
want=$(printf '%s' "$2" | tr -d ' ' | tr '[:lower:]' '[:upper:]')
dest="$3"
[[ "$want" =~ ^[0-9A-F]{40}$ ]] || { echo "pin-repo-key: fingerprint must be 40 hex digits" >&2; exit 2; }
command -v gpg >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "pin-repo-key: gpg is not installed" >&2; exit 1; }

work=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$work"' EXIT
chmod 0700 "$work"
curl -fsSL --max-time 60 "$url" -o "${work}/key"
export GNUPGHOME="${work}/gnupg"
install -d -m 0700 "$GNUPGHOME"
gpg --batch --quiet --import "${work}/key" 2>/dev/null
if ! gpg --batch --with-colons --list-keys 2>/dev/null \
     | awk -F: '$1 == "pub" { g = 1; next } $1 == "fpr" && g { print $10; g = 0 }' | grep -qx "$want"; then
  echo "pin-repo-key: ${url} has no key with fingerprint ${want}; not installed" >&2
  exit 1
fi
gpg --batch --export --armor "$want" > "${work}/out" 2>/dev/null
[[ -s "${work}/out" ]] || { echo "pin-repo-key: export failed" >&2; exit 1; }
install -D -m 0644 "${work}/out" "$dest"
echo "pin-repo-key: ${dest} installed (${want})"
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 /usr/local/sbin/pin-repo-key

install -d "$(dirname /etc/sysctl.d/90-ci-hardening.conf)"
cat > /etc/sysctl.d/90-ci-hardening.conf <<'__SIC_EOF__'
# Kernel hardening for runner hosts. A leading "-" ignores a key the running kernel does not have.
-kernel.kptr_restrict = 2
-kernel.dmesg_restrict = 1
-kernel.yama.ptrace_scope = 1
-fs.protected_fifos = 2
-fs.protected_regular = 2
net.ipv4.conf.all.log_martians = 1
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/sysctl.d/90-ci-hardening.conf

install -d "$(dirname /etc/security/limits.d/50-nocore.conf)"
cat > /etc/security/limits.d/50-nocore.conf <<'__SIC_EOF__'
* hard core 0
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/security/limits.d/50-nocore.conf

install -d "$(dirname /etc/systemd/coredump.conf.d/50-off.conf)"
cat > /etc/systemd/coredump.conf.d/50-off.conf <<'__SIC_EOF__'
[Coredump]
Storage=none
ProcessSizeMax=0
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/systemd/coredump.conf.d/50-off.conf

install -d "$(dirname /etc/profile.d/90-shell-history.sh)"
cat > /etc/profile.d/90-shell-history.sh <<'__SIC_EOF__'
# shellcheck shell=bash
# Shell history and fzf key bindings for interactive bash (Ctrl+R history search, Ctrl+T file search).
# Installed as /etc/profile.d/90-shell-history.sh (runner hosts) or 90-server-shell.sh (plain servers).
# The same text is embedded in the server/server-init-*.sh scripts (they must stay self-contained):
# keep all copies identical, tools/test-cloud-init.py checks that.
[ -n "${BASH_VERSION-}" ] || return 0
[[ $- == *i* ]] || return 0

__shell_history_vars() {
  HISTSIZE=100000
  HISTFILESIZE=200000
  HISTTIMEFORMAT='%F %T  '
  HISTCONTROL=ignoreboth:erasedups
}
__shell_history_vars
shopt -s histappend cmdhist

# Run before each prompt: append the last command to the history file so parallel sessions share history.
# On Debian and Ubuntu the user's ~/.bashrc runs after this file and lowers the limits, so the first
# prompt applies them again (a later change by hand in a session is kept).
__shell_history_prompt() {
  history -a
  if [[ -z "${__shell_history_done-}" ]]; then
    __shell_history_done=1
    __shell_history_vars
  fi
}
# Keeps an existing PROMPT_COMMAND (string or array) and is safe to source twice.
# When PROMPT_COMMAND is still empty (Fedora/EL login shells read profile.d before /etc/bashrc, which sets
# the terminal title only if PROMPT_COMMAND is empty), use an array with an empty first element: /etc/bashrc
# then fills element 0 with its title command and the hook stays in element 1 (bash 5.1+ runs every element).
if [[ -z "${PROMPT_COMMAND-}" && "$(declare -p PROMPT_COMMAND 2>/dev/null)" != "declare -a"* ]] && (( BASH_VERSINFO[0] > 5 || (BASH_VERSINFO[0] == 5 && BASH_VERSINFO[1] >= 1) )); then
  unset PROMPT_COMMAND
  PROMPT_COMMAND=('' '__shell_history_prompt')
fi
case "$(declare -p PROMPT_COMMAND 2>/dev/null)" in
  "declare -a"*)
    [[ " ${PROMPT_COMMAND[*]} " == *" __shell_history_prompt "* ]] || PROMPT_COMMAND+=('__shell_history_prompt')
    ;;
  *)
    if [[ "${PROMPT_COMMAND-}" != *__shell_history_prompt* ]]; then
      __pc="${PROMPT_COMMAND-}"
      while [[ "$__pc" == *[[:space:]\;] ]]; do __pc="${__pc%?}"; done
      # shellcheck disable=SC2178
      PROMPT_COMMAND="${__pc:+$__pc;}__shell_history_prompt"
      unset __pc
    fi
    ;;
esac

# Debian and Ubuntu install fd as fdfind.
if ! command -v fd >/dev/null 2>&1 && command -v fdfind >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  alias fd=fdfind
  __fd=fdfind
elif command -v fd >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  __fd=fd
fi
if [[ -n "${__fd-}" ]]; then
  export FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND="${FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND:-$__fd --type f --hidden --follow --exclude .git}"
  export FZF_CTRL_T_COMMAND="${FZF_CTRL_T_COMMAND:-$FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND}"
  export FZF_ALT_C_COMMAND="${FZF_ALT_C_COMMAND:-$__fd --type d --hidden --follow --exclude .git}"
fi
unset __fd

if command -v fzf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  for __f in /usr/share/fzf/shell/key-bindings.bash /usr/share/doc/fzf/examples/key-bindings.bash; do
    if [[ -r "$__f" ]]; then
      # shellcheck source=/dev/null
      . "$__f"
      __fzf_done=1
      break
    fi
  done
  # fzf 0.48+ can print its own bindings when the package ships no file.
  if [[ -z "${__fzf_done-}" ]] && __fzf_b=$(fzf --bash 2>/dev/null); then
    eval "$__fzf_b"
  fi
  unset __f __fzf_done __fzf_b
fi
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 /etc/profile.d/90-shell-history.sh

restorecon -R /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d /etc/docker /etc/gitlab-runner /etc/profile.d /usr/local/sbin /etc/yum.repos.d /etc/sysctl.d
sysctl --system
sh -c 'if sshd -t; then systemctl reload sshd; else rm -f /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/00-ci-hardening.conf; echo ssh-dropin-rejected-defaults-kept >> /var/log/ci-bootstrap-failed.log; fi'
sh -c 'dnf -y -q install epel-release || echo optional-tool-missing-epel >> /var/log/ci-bootstrap-failed.log; for p in htop btop ncdu iotop mtr nano vim-enhanced rsync wget unzip tar bash-completion skopeo dnf-utils mc fd-find ripgrep fzf; do dnf -y -q install $p || echo optional-tool-missing-$p >> /var/log/ci-bootstrap-failed.log; done'
sh -c 'if [ -f /etc/dnf/automatic.conf ]; then sed -i -E '"'"'s/^upgrade_type = .*/upgrade_type = security/; s/^apply_updates = .*/apply_updates = yes/'"'"' /etc/dnf/automatic.conf && systemctl enable --now dnf-automatic.timer; else echo dnf-automatic-config-not-found >> /var/log/ci-bootstrap-failed.log; fi'
systemctl restart systemd-journald
timedatectl set-timezone Europe/Kyiv
pin-repo-key https://download.docker.com/linux/rhel/gpg 060A61C51B558A7F742B77AAC52FEB6B621E9F35 /etc/pki/rpm-gpg/docker-ce.asc
dnf -y -q install docker-ce docker-ce-cli containerd.io docker-buildx-plugin docker-compose-plugin
systemctl enable --now docker
pin-repo-key https://packages.gitlab.com/runner/gitlab-runner/gpgkey F6403F6544A38863DAA0B6E03F01618A51312F3F /etc/pki/rpm-gpg/gitlab-runner.asc
pin-repo-key https://packages.gitlab.com/gpgkey/runner/49F16C5CC3A0F81F.pub.gpg 931DA69CFA3AFEBBC97DAA8C6C57C29C6BA75A4E /etc/pki/rpm-gpg/gitlab-runner-pkg.asc
dnf -y -q install gitlab-runner
usermod -aG docker gitlab-runner
chmod 600 /etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
systemctl restart gitlab-runner
sh -c 'for s in sshd docker gitlab-runner; do systemctl is-active --quiet $s || echo bootstrap-check-failed-$s >> /var/log/ci-bootstrap-failed.log; done'

systemctl is-active docker gitlab-runner
INSTALL
````

<a id="users"></a>
## Users: admin, service accounts, SSH hardening

Creates the admin user `ops` with sudo without password and the keys from root, the service accounts `gitlab-runner` (the runner service then runs as it) and `monitoring` (runs the monitoring and logging containers), and the SSH drop-in: keys only, only the `ssh-users` group. Root keeps SSH by key until the [Lock root SSH](#lock-root) block. Run it first; safe to rerun.

````bash
sudo bash <<'INSTALL'
set -Eeuo pipefail
R=/root/server-init-config

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/lib.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Shared helpers for the installers (runner/install, server and common). Sourced, not executed.
# shellcheck shell=bash

REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
export REPO_ROOT

log() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

require_root() { [[ $EUID -eq 0 ]] || die "run as root"; }

selinux_on() { command -v selinuxenabled >/dev/null 2>&1 && selinuxenabled; }

pkg_install() {
  if command -v dnf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    dnf -y -q install "$@"
  elif command -v apt-get >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    apt-get update -q && apt-get install -y -q "$@"
  else
    die "no supported package manager"
  fi
}

# Label a directory so containers may read files in it (no-op without SELinux).
selinux_label_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  install -d -m 0755 "$dir"
  selinux_on || return 0
  command -v semanage >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_install policycoreutils-python-utils
  # Read the whole list first: grep -q exits at the first match and would SIGPIPE the producer under pipefail.
  if ! grep -q "^${dir}(/\.\*)?" <<<"$(semanage fcontext -l)"; then
    semanage fcontext -a -t container_file_t "${dir}(/.*)?"
  fi
  restorecon -R "$dir"
}

restorecon_if() { if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon "$@" || true; fi; }

# RHEL-family sudo resets PATH to secure_path, which can leave out /usr/local/bin (so `sudo health` fails).
# Adds a validated drop-in unless some sudoers file already sets a secure_path that includes it.
ensure_secure_path() {
  local f=/etc/sudoers.d/50-secure-path tmp
  command -v visudo >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  if grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path[[:space:]]*=.*/usr/local/bin' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0
  fi
  if ! grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0   # no secure_path at all (Debian family keeps it in sudoers; others use the caller PATH)
  fi
  tmp=$(mktemp)
  printf 'Defaults secure_path="/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"\n' > "$tmp"
  if visudo -cqf "$tmp"; then
    install -d -m 0750 /etc/sudoers.d
    install -m 0440 -o root -g root "$tmp" "$f"
    log "sudo secure_path now includes /usr/local/bin (${f})"
  else
    echo "WARN: generated sudoers drop-in is invalid, secure_path left as it was" >&2
  fi
  rm -f "$tmp"
}

ci_jobs_running() { docker ps -q --filter "label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true" 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }

require_runner_idle() {
  local n
  n=$(ci_jobs_running)
  (( n == 0 )) || die "${n} CI job container(s) running, retry when the runner is idle"
}

# Timestamped copy before a change. Skipped when an identical backup already exists, so reruns of an idempotent
# installer do not pile up copies of an unchanged file.
backup() {
  local f=$1 b
  for b in "${f}".bak.*; do
    [[ -f "$b" ]] && cmp -s "$f" "$b" && return 0
  done
  cp -a "$f" "${f}.bak.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
}

# Prometheus scrape jobs for the logging stack, shared by runner/install/logging.sh and runner/install/update.sh.
prom_jobs_logging() {
  cat <<'YAML'
  - job_name: loki
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:3100"]
  - job_name: alloy
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:12345"]
YAML
}

# Merge KEY=VALUE entries into the single [[runners]] environment line of config.toml.
runner_env_merge() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml existing list key e line
  [[ $(grep -c '^\[\[runners\]\]' "$cfg") -eq 1 ]] || die "expected exactly one [[runners]] block in $cfg"
  existing=$(grep -E '^  environment = \[' "$cfg" | sed -E 's/^  environment = \[(.*)\]$/\1/' || true)
  list="$existing"
  for e in "$@"; do
    key="${e%%=*}"
    if grep -q "\"${key}=" <<<"$list"; then
      list=$(sed -E "s|\"${key}=[^\"]*\"|\"${e}\"|" <<<"$list")
    else
      list="${list:+${list}, }\"${e}\""
    fi
  done
  line="  environment = [${list}]"
  if grep -qE '^  environment = \[' "$cfg"; then
    sed -i -E "s|^  environment = \[.*\]$|${line}|" "$cfg"
  else
    sed -i -E "0,/^  executor = .*/s||${line}\n&|" "$cfg"
  fi
  chmod 600 "$cfg"
  runner_cfg_owner
}

# When the runner service runs as gitlab-runner (runner/install/users.sh), it must own its config after root edits it.
runner_cfg_owner() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
  if [[ -f /etc/systemd/system/gitlab-runner.service.d/10-service-user.conf ]] && id gitlab-runner >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    chown -R gitlab-runner:gitlab-runner /etc/gitlab-runner
    chmod 600 "$cfg"
  fi
}

# --- service user for the monitoring and logging stacks (created by runner/install/users.sh) ---------------
MON_USER="${MON_USER:-monitoring}"

# Set MON_UID and MON_GID, or stop if the service user is missing.
mon_ids() {
  id "$MON_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "service user ${MON_USER} missing, run runner/install/users.sh first"
  MON_UID=$(id -u "$MON_USER")
  MON_GID=$(id -g "$MON_USER")
}

# env_set FILE KEY VALUE: set KEY=VALUE in a root-only env file.
env_set() {
  local f="$1" k="$2" v="$3"
  touch "$f"
  chmod 600 "$f"
  if grep -q "^${k}=" "$f"; then
    sed -i "s|^${k}=.*|${k}=${v}|" "$f"
  else
    printf '%s=%s\n' "$k" "$v" >> "$f"
  fi
}

# stack_ids DIR [logging]: write the ids the compose file needs into DIR/.env.
stack_ids() {
  local dir="$1" docker_gid journal_gid
  mon_ids
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_UID "$MON_UID"
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_GID "$MON_GID"
  if [[ "${2:-}" == logging ]]; then
    docker_gid=$(getent group docker | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$docker_gid" ]] || die "docker group missing"
    env_set "${dir}/.env" DOCKER_GID "$docker_gid"
    journal_gid=$(getent group systemd-journal | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$journal_gid" ]] || die "systemd-journal group missing"
    # compose rejects equal group_add items, so the journal group is only added when it differs from docker's.
    if [[ "$journal_gid" != "$docker_gid" ]]; then
      printf 'services:\n  alloy:\n    group_add:\n      - "%s"\n' "$journal_gid" > "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    else
      rm -f "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    fi
  fi
}

# Config readable by the service user, writable by root only; .env (secrets) stays root-only.
own_stack_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  chown -R "root:${MON_USER}" "$dir"
  chmod -R u=rwX,g=rX,o= "$dir"
  if [[ -f "${dir}/.env" ]]; then
    chown root:root "${dir}/.env"
    chmod 600 "${dir}/.env"
  fi
}

# Create the stack's containers and volumes without starting them, then hand the volumes to the service user.
chown_volumes() {
  local dir="$1" project v
  mon_ids
  project=$(cd "$dir" && docker compose config --format json | jq -r .name)
  (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start --quiet-pull >/dev/null 2>&1) || (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start >/dev/null)
  for v in $(docker volume ls -q --filter "label=com.docker.compose.project=${project}"); do
    docker run --rm --network none -v "${v}:/v" alpine:3.20 chown -R "${MON_UID}:${MON_GID}" /v
  done
}
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/lib.sh"

install -d "$R/runner/install"
cat > "$R/runner/install/users.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Users and SSH access for the runner host. Self-contained (no lib.sh) so cloud-init can embed it.
#
#   admin          ops (ADMIN_USER): SSH by key, sudo without password, not in the docker group
#   gitlab-runner  service account: no shell, no password, no SSH; the runner service runs as this user
#                  with access to Docker through the docker group (root-equivalent, needed by the executor)
#   monitoring     service account: no shell, no password, no SSH; owns the monitoring and logging containers
#
# SSH: keys only, only members of ssh-users, port SSH_PORT (default 22; remembered from the existing drop-in), no agent or X11 forwarding,
# local port forwarding kept for tunnels.
# Root keeps SSH by key until --lock-root, which refuses to run while the admin has no key. With another
# SSH_PORT, sshd listens on 22 and SSH_PORT until --lock-root, which then closes 22. A locked server stays
# locked on rerun unless --unlock-root is given. Safe to rerun.
set -Eeuo pipefail

ADMIN_USER="${ADMIN_USER:-ops}"
MON_USER="${MON_USER:-monitoring}"
RUNNER_USER=gitlab-runner
SSH_GROUP="${SSH_GROUP:-ssh-users}"
DROPIN=/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/00-ci-hardening.conf
# SSH_PORT from the environment wins; otherwise the port written by the previous run is kept, so a rerun,
# --lock-root and --unlock-root never fall back to 22 on a host that listens elsewhere.
if [[ -z "${SSH_PORT:-}" ]]; then
  SSH_PORT=$(awk 'tolower($1) == "port" && $2 != 22 { p = $2 } END { print p }' "$DROPIN" 2>/dev/null || true)
fi
SSH_PORT="${SSH_PORT:-22}"
RUNNER_DROPIN=/etc/systemd/system/gitlab-runner.service.d/10-service-user.conf
RUNNER_CFG=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
LOCK_ROOT=0
UNLOCK_ROOT=0
RELOAD=1

log() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

usage() {
  cat <<USAGE
Usage: $0 [--lock-root | --unlock-root] [--no-reload]

Creates the admin user (ADMIN_USER, default ops), the service
accounts (${MON_USER}, ${RUNNER_USER} if installed), the ${SSH_GROUP} group and the SSH drop-in ${DROPIN}.
The admin gets the keys from /root/.ssh/authorized_keys.

  --lock-root    close root SSH (and port 22 when SSH_PORT is another port). Run it only after
                 "ssh ADMIN@server sudo -n true" works from your laptop.
  --unlock-root  undo --lock-root (root by key again)
  --no-reload    write the config but do not reload sshd or restart gitlab-runner

Env: ADMIN_USER, MON_USER (default monitoring), SSH_GROUP (default ssh-users),
     SSH_PORT (default 22, or the port already in ${DROPIN}; give it once, reruns keep it)
Rollback: delete ${DROPIN} and ${RUNNER_DROPIN}, then: systemctl reload sshd; systemctl daemon-reload; systemctl restart gitlab-runner
USAGE
}

while (( $# )); do
  case "$1" in
    --lock-root) LOCK_ROOT=1 ;;
    --unlock-root) UNLOCK_ROOT=1 ;;
    --no-reload) RELOAD=0 ;;
    -h|--help) usage; exit 0 ;;
    *) usage >&2; die "unknown option: $1" ;;
  esac
  shift
done

[[ $EUID -eq 0 ]] || die "run as root (sudo $0)"
(( LOCK_ROOT && UNLOCK_ROOT )) && die "--lock-root and --unlock-root exclude each other"
if ! [[ "$SSH_PORT" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || (( SSH_PORT < 1 || SSH_PORT > 65535 )); then die "invalid SSH_PORT: ${SSH_PORT}"; fi
[[ "$ADMIN_USER" =~ ^[a-z_][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$ ]] || die "invalid ADMIN_USER: '${ADMIN_USER}'"
[[ "$ADMIN_USER" != root && "$ADMIN_USER" != "$MON_USER" && "$ADMIN_USER" != "$RUNNER_USER" ]] || die "ADMIN_USER must be a separate user"

NOLOGIN=$(command -v nologin || true)
[[ -n "$NOLOGIN" ]] || NOLOGIN=/sbin/nologin
if getent group wheel >/dev/null; then
  SUDO_GROUP=wheel
elif getent group sudo >/dev/null; then
  SUDO_GROUP=sudo
else
  die "neither wheel nor sudo group exists"
fi
command -v visudo >/dev/null || die "sudo is not installed"

# --- admin -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
getent group "$SSH_GROUP" >/dev/null || groupadd -r "$SSH_GROUP"
if id "$ADMIN_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  usermod -aG "${SUDO_GROUP},${SSH_GROUP}" "$ADMIN_USER"
else
  useradd -m -s /bin/bash -G "${SUDO_GROUP},${SSH_GROUP}" -c "server admin" "$ADMIN_USER"
  log "created admin user ${ADMIN_USER}"
fi
# "!" (locked) can block key logins when sshd runs without PAM; "*" means no password, key login allowed.
if [[ "$(getent shadow "$ADMIN_USER" | cut -d: -f2)" == !* ]]; then
  usermod -p '*' "$ADMIN_USER"
fi
if id -nG "$ADMIN_USER" | tr ' ' '\n' | grep -qx docker; then
  gpasswd -d "$ADMIN_USER" docker >/dev/null
  log "removed ${ADMIN_USER} from the docker group (root-equivalent); use sudo docker"
fi

sudoers="/etc/sudoers.d/90-${ADMIN_USER}"
tmp=$(mktemp)
# secure_path: the Rocky default can leave out /usr/local/bin, where health and the cleanup scripts live.
printf '%s ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD:ALL\nDefaults:%s secure_path="/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"\n' \
  "$ADMIN_USER" "$ADMIN_USER" > "$tmp"
visudo -cqf "$tmp" || { rm -f "$tmp"; die "generated sudoers file is invalid"; }
install -m 0440 -o root -g root "$tmp" "$sudoers"
rm -f "$tmp"

home=$(getent passwd "$ADMIN_USER" | cut -d: -f6)
group=$(id -gn "$ADMIN_USER")
ak="${home}/.ssh/authorized_keys"
install -d -m 0700 -o "$ADMIN_USER" -g "$group" "${home}/.ssh"
touch "$ak"
if [[ -f /root/.ssh/authorized_keys ]]; then
  # Plain key lines only: lines with options (for example cloud-init's "Please login as" command=) are skipped.
  while IFS= read -r line; do
    grep -qxF -- "$line" "$ak" || printf '%s\n' "$line" >> "$ak"
  done < <(grep -E '^(ssh-(ed25519|rsa)|ecdsa-sha2-|sk-)' /root/.ssh/authorized_keys || true)
fi
chown "$ADMIN_USER:$group" "$ak"
chmod 0600 "$ak"
if [[ -f /root/.tmux.conf && ! -f "${home}/.tmux.conf" ]]; then
  install -m 0644 -o "$ADMIN_USER" -g "$group" /root/.tmux.conf "${home}/.tmux.conf"
fi
if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon -R "${home}/.ssh" || true; fi
keys=$(grep -cE '^(ssh-|ecdsa-|sk-)' "$ak" || true)
log "admin ${ADMIN_USER}: groups $(id -nG "$ADMIN_USER" | tr ' ' ','), ${keys:-0} SSH key(s), sudo without password"

# --- service accounts --------------------------------------------------------------------------
if ! id "$MON_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  useradd -r -U -M -d /nonexistent -s "$NOLOGIN" -c "monitoring and logging containers" "$MON_USER"
  log "created service user ${MON_USER}"
fi
usermod -s "$NOLOGIN" "$MON_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
log "service ${MON_USER}: uid $(id -u "$MON_USER"), gid $(id -g "$MON_USER"), shell ${NOLOGIN}"

if id "$RUNNER_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  usermod -s "$NOLOGIN" "$RUNNER_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  usermod -L "$RUNNER_USER" 2>/dev/null || true
  getent group docker >/dev/null && usermod -aG docker "$RUNNER_USER"
  install -d -m 0755 "$(dirname "$RUNNER_DROPIN")"
  cat > "${RUNNER_DROPIN}.new" <<'EOF'
# Run the runner service as its own user instead of root. Docker access comes from the docker group.
[Service]
User=gitlab-runner
Group=gitlab-runner
SupplementaryGroups=docker
ExecStart=
ExecStart=/usr/bin/gitlab-runner run --working-directory /home/gitlab-runner --config /etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml --service gitlab-runner
EOF
  changed=0
  if ! cmp -s "${RUNNER_DROPIN}.new" "$RUNNER_DROPIN" 2>/dev/null; then
    mv "${RUNNER_DROPIN}.new" "$RUNNER_DROPIN"
    chmod 0644 "$RUNNER_DROPIN"
    changed=1
  else
    rm -f "${RUNNER_DROPIN}.new"
  fi
  # The service reads and rewrites config.toml (token, system id), so it owns /etc/gitlab-runner and its home.
  install -d -m 0700 -o "$RUNNER_USER" -g "$RUNNER_USER" /etc/gitlab-runner
  chown -R "$RUNNER_USER:$RUNNER_USER" /etc/gitlab-runner
  if [[ -f "$RUNNER_CFG" ]]; then chmod 0600 "$RUNNER_CFG"; fi
  install -d -m 0750 -o "$RUNNER_USER" -g "$RUNNER_USER" "/home/${RUNNER_USER}"
  chown -R "$RUNNER_USER:$RUNNER_USER" "/home/${RUNNER_USER}"
  if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon -R /etc/gitlab-runner "$(dirname "$RUNNER_DROPIN")" || true; fi
  if (( RELOAD )); then
    systemctl daemon-reload
    restart=0
    if (( changed )) || [[ "$(ps -o user= -p "$(systemctl show -p MainPID --value gitlab-runner)" 2>/dev/null | tr -d ' ')" != "$RUNNER_USER" ]]; then
      restart=1
    fi
    if (( restart )); then
      main_pid=$(systemctl show -p MainPID --value gitlab-runner)
      if [[ "${main_pid:-0}" == 0 ]]; then
        # Stopped service: nothing to interrupt, and the idle gate would only block the start.
        systemctl start gitlab-runner
        sleep 3
      elif ! job_ids=$(docker ps -q --filter "label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true" 2>/dev/null); then
        # A docker failure is not "0 jobs": restarting could kill running CI jobs.
        log "WARN: cannot confirm that no CI job is running (docker does not answer), gitlab-runner not restarted; check with: docker ps --filter label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true, then: systemctl restart gitlab-runner"
      elif [[ -n "$job_ids" ]]; then
        log "WARN: $(wc -w <<<"$job_ids") CI job(s) running, gitlab-runner not restarted; run: systemctl restart gitlab-runner"
      else
        systemctl restart gitlab-runner
        sleep 3
      fi
    fi
    pid=$(systemctl show -p MainPID --value gitlab-runner)
    if [[ "${pid:-0}" != 0 ]]; then
      log "service ${RUNNER_USER}: runner process runs as $(ps -o user= -p "$pid" | tr -d ' ') (groups: $(ps -o supgrp= -p "$pid" | tr -d ' '))"
    else
      log "WARN: gitlab-runner is not running: journalctl -u gitlab-runner -n 30"
    fi
  fi
else
  log "${RUNNER_USER} not installed yet, rerun this script after installing GitLab Runner"
fi

# --- SSH ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A server that is already locked stays locked, so a rerun never reopens root or port 22 by accident.
if (( ! LOCK_ROOT && ! UNLOCK_ROOT )) && grep -qsx 'PermitRootLogin no' "$DROPIN"; then
  LOCK_ROOT=1
  log "keeping the existing root lock (use --unlock-root to undo it)"
fi
if (( LOCK_ROOT )) && (( ${keys:-0} == 0 )); then
  die "--lock-root refused: ${ADMIN_USER} has no SSH key, root would be the only way in"
fi
if (( LOCK_ROOT )); then
  root_login=no
  allow="AllowGroups ${SSH_GROUP}"
  ports="Port ${SSH_PORT}"
else
  root_login=prohibit-password
  allow="AllowGroups ${SSH_GROUP} root"
  ports="Port 22"
  if [[ "$SSH_PORT" != 22 ]]; then ports+=$'\n'"Port ${SSH_PORT}"; fi
fi

# SELinux only lets sshd bind ports labeled ssh_port_t; firewalld (if active) must allow the port too.
if [[ "$SSH_PORT" != 22 ]] && command -v selinuxenabled >/dev/null 2>&1 && selinuxenabled; then
  command -v semanage >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "semanage missing: install policycoreutils-python-utils"
  if ! semanage port -l | awk '$1 == "ssh_port_t" { for (i = 3; i <= NF; i++) print $i }' | tr -d ',' | grep -qx "$SSH_PORT"; then
    semanage port -a -t ssh_port_t -p tcp "$SSH_PORT" 2>/dev/null || semanage port -m -t ssh_port_t -p tcp "$SSH_PORT"
    log "SELinux: port ${SSH_PORT} labeled ssh_port_t"
  fi
fi
if systemctl is-active --quiet firewalld 2>/dev/null; then
  firewall-cmd -q --permanent --add-port="${SSH_PORT}/tcp" && firewall-cmd -q --reload
  log "firewalld: port ${SSH_PORT}/tcp allowed"
fi
# Debian runner hosts get ufw from host-firewall.sh: the new port must be open there too, or sshd listens on a port the
# firewall drops (and --lock-root, which closes port 22, would then lock you out).
if command -v ufw >/dev/null 2>&1 && ufw status 2>/dev/null | grep -q '^Status: active'; then
  ufw allow "${SSH_PORT}/tcp" >/dev/null || die "cannot allow ${SSH_PORT}/tcp in ufw"
  log "ufw: port ${SSH_PORT}/tcp allowed"
fi
# Add this port to the fail2ban sshd jail written by host-firewall.sh.
JAIL_FILE="${JAIL_FILE:-/etc/fail2ban/jail.d/50-sshd.local}"
if [[ -f "$JAIL_FILE" ]] && grep -q '^port ' "$JAIL_FILE" && ! grep -E '^port ' "$JAIL_FILE" | tr -c '0-9\n' ' ' | tr ' ' '\n' | grep -qx "$SSH_PORT"; then
  sed -i -E "s/^(port[[:space:]]*=[[:space:]]*.*)\$/\1,${SSH_PORT}/" "$JAIL_FILE"
  systemctl reload fail2ban >/dev/null 2>&1 || systemctl restart fail2ban >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  log "fail2ban: sshd jail now also watches port ${SSH_PORT}"
fi
# Never write a config that leaves SSH only on a port the running host firewall drops.
if [[ "$SSH_PORT" != 22 ]]; then
  if systemctl is-active --quiet firewalld 2>/dev/null && ! firewall-cmd --query-port="${SSH_PORT}/tcp" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    die "firewalld does not allow ${SSH_PORT}/tcp in the running rules; sshd config left unchanged"
  fi
  if command -v ufw >/dev/null 2>&1 && ufw status 2>/dev/null | grep -q '^Status: active' \
     && ! ufw status 2>/dev/null | grep -qE "^${SSH_PORT}/tcp([[:space:]]+\(v6\))?[[:space:]]+(ALLOW|LIMIT)"; then
    die "ufw does not allow ${SSH_PORT}/tcp in the running rules; sshd config left unchanged"
  fi
fi
[[ -d /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d ]] || die "/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d missing (OpenSSH too old for drop-ins?)"
grep -qiE '^\s*Include\s+/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/' /etc/ssh/sshd_config || die "/etc/ssh/sshd_config does not include sshd_config.d"

new="${DROPIN}.new"
cat > "$new" <<EOF
# Managed by runner/install/users.sh. First match wins in sshd, so this file is named 00- to load first.
${ports}
PasswordAuthentication no
KbdInteractiveAuthentication no
PubkeyAuthentication yes
PermitRootLogin ${root_login}
${allow}
MaxAuthTries 3
LoginGraceTime 30
ClientAliveInterval 300
ClientAliveCountMax 2
X11Forwarding no
AllowAgentForwarding no
AllowTcpForwarding local
PermitTunnel no
EOF
chmod 0600 "$new"
old_backup=""
if [[ -f "$DROPIN" ]] && cmp -s "$new" "$DROPIN"; then
  rm -f "$new"   # same content: no backup, no rewrite, a rerun leaves the managed state untouched
else
  if [[ -f "$DROPIN" ]]; then
    old_backup="${DROPIN}.bak.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    cp -a "$DROPIN" "$old_backup"
  fi
  mv "$new" "$DROPIN"
fi
legacy=/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/60-ci-hardening.conf
[[ -f "$legacy" ]] && mv "$legacy" "${legacy}.disabled"
if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/* 2>/dev/null || true; fi

sshd_bin=$(command -v sshd || echo /usr/sbin/sshd)
if ! "$sshd_bin" -t; then
  if [[ -n "$old_backup" ]]; then mv "$old_backup" "$DROPIN"; else rm -f "$DROPIN"; fi
  [[ -f "${legacy}.disabled" ]] && mv "${legacy}.disabled" "$legacy"
  die "sshd rejected the new config, previous config restored"
fi
if (( RELOAD )); then
  if systemctl is-enabled --quiet ssh.socket 2>/dev/null; then
    # Socket activation (Ubuntu, some Debian setups): the socket generator reads Port from sshd_config.
    systemctl daemon-reload
    systemctl restart ssh.socket
  elif systemctl cat sshd >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    systemctl reload sshd
  else
    systemctl reload ssh
  fi
fi
effective=$("$sshd_bin" -T 2>/dev/null | grep -Ei '^(port|permitrootlogin|passwordauthentication|allowgroups|allowtcpforwarding) ' | tr '\n' ';' || true)
log "sshd: ${effective}"

port_opt=""
if [[ "$SSH_PORT" != 22 ]]; then port_opt="-p ${SSH_PORT} "; fi
if (( LOCK_ROOT )); then
  log "OK: root SSH closed. Log in as: ssh ${port_opt}${ADMIN_USER}@<server>"
else
  if [[ -n "$port_opt" ]]; then log "OK: allow TCP ${SSH_PORT} in the Hetzner Firewall first"; fi
  log "OK: test from your laptop:  ssh ${port_opt}${ADMIN_USER}@<server> 'sudo -n true && echo sudo works'"
  log "    then lock root:         sudo $0 --lock-root"
fi
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/runner/install/users.sh"

echo "files written to $R: 2"
bash "$R/runner/install/users.sh" </dev/null
INSTALL
````

<a id="packages"></a>
## Admin tools: htop, btop, ncdu, iotop and others

htop, btop, ncdu, iotop, mtr, nano, vim, rsync, wget, unzip, bash-completion, skopeo, dig, lsof, sysstat (`sar`), tcpdump, traceroute, nc, jq, git, tmux, mc, fd, rg, fzf, plus the shell aliases, shell history with fzf Ctrl+R and tmux config. For a server created with an older init; safe to rerun.

````bash
sudo bash <<'INSTALL'
set -Eeuo pipefail
R=/root/server-init-config

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/lib.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Shared helpers for the installers (runner/install, server and common). Sourced, not executed.
# shellcheck shell=bash

REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
export REPO_ROOT

log() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

require_root() { [[ $EUID -eq 0 ]] || die "run as root"; }

selinux_on() { command -v selinuxenabled >/dev/null 2>&1 && selinuxenabled; }

pkg_install() {
  if command -v dnf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    dnf -y -q install "$@"
  elif command -v apt-get >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    apt-get update -q && apt-get install -y -q "$@"
  else
    die "no supported package manager"
  fi
}

# Label a directory so containers may read files in it (no-op without SELinux).
selinux_label_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  install -d -m 0755 "$dir"
  selinux_on || return 0
  command -v semanage >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_install policycoreutils-python-utils
  # Read the whole list first: grep -q exits at the first match and would SIGPIPE the producer under pipefail.
  if ! grep -q "^${dir}(/\.\*)?" <<<"$(semanage fcontext -l)"; then
    semanage fcontext -a -t container_file_t "${dir}(/.*)?"
  fi
  restorecon -R "$dir"
}

restorecon_if() { if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon "$@" || true; fi; }

# RHEL-family sudo resets PATH to secure_path, which can leave out /usr/local/bin (so `sudo health` fails).
# Adds a validated drop-in unless some sudoers file already sets a secure_path that includes it.
ensure_secure_path() {
  local f=/etc/sudoers.d/50-secure-path tmp
  command -v visudo >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  if grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path[[:space:]]*=.*/usr/local/bin' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0
  fi
  if ! grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0   # no secure_path at all (Debian family keeps it in sudoers; others use the caller PATH)
  fi
  tmp=$(mktemp)
  printf 'Defaults secure_path="/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"\n' > "$tmp"
  if visudo -cqf "$tmp"; then
    install -d -m 0750 /etc/sudoers.d
    install -m 0440 -o root -g root "$tmp" "$f"
    log "sudo secure_path now includes /usr/local/bin (${f})"
  else
    echo "WARN: generated sudoers drop-in is invalid, secure_path left as it was" >&2
  fi
  rm -f "$tmp"
}

ci_jobs_running() { docker ps -q --filter "label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true" 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }

require_runner_idle() {
  local n
  n=$(ci_jobs_running)
  (( n == 0 )) || die "${n} CI job container(s) running, retry when the runner is idle"
}

# Timestamped copy before a change. Skipped when an identical backup already exists, so reruns of an idempotent
# installer do not pile up copies of an unchanged file.
backup() {
  local f=$1 b
  for b in "${f}".bak.*; do
    [[ -f "$b" ]] && cmp -s "$f" "$b" && return 0
  done
  cp -a "$f" "${f}.bak.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
}

# Prometheus scrape jobs for the logging stack, shared by runner/install/logging.sh and runner/install/update.sh.
prom_jobs_logging() {
  cat <<'YAML'
  - job_name: loki
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:3100"]
  - job_name: alloy
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:12345"]
YAML
}

# Merge KEY=VALUE entries into the single [[runners]] environment line of config.toml.
runner_env_merge() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml existing list key e line
  [[ $(grep -c '^\[\[runners\]\]' "$cfg") -eq 1 ]] || die "expected exactly one [[runners]] block in $cfg"
  existing=$(grep -E '^  environment = \[' "$cfg" | sed -E 's/^  environment = \[(.*)\]$/\1/' || true)
  list="$existing"
  for e in "$@"; do
    key="${e%%=*}"
    if grep -q "\"${key}=" <<<"$list"; then
      list=$(sed -E "s|\"${key}=[^\"]*\"|\"${e}\"|" <<<"$list")
    else
      list="${list:+${list}, }\"${e}\""
    fi
  done
  line="  environment = [${list}]"
  if grep -qE '^  environment = \[' "$cfg"; then
    sed -i -E "s|^  environment = \[.*\]$|${line}|" "$cfg"
  else
    sed -i -E "0,/^  executor = .*/s||${line}\n&|" "$cfg"
  fi
  chmod 600 "$cfg"
  runner_cfg_owner
}

# When the runner service runs as gitlab-runner (runner/install/users.sh), it must own its config after root edits it.
runner_cfg_owner() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
  if [[ -f /etc/systemd/system/gitlab-runner.service.d/10-service-user.conf ]] && id gitlab-runner >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    chown -R gitlab-runner:gitlab-runner /etc/gitlab-runner
    chmod 600 "$cfg"
  fi
}

# --- service user for the monitoring and logging stacks (created by runner/install/users.sh) ---------------
MON_USER="${MON_USER:-monitoring}"

# Set MON_UID and MON_GID, or stop if the service user is missing.
mon_ids() {
  id "$MON_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "service user ${MON_USER} missing, run runner/install/users.sh first"
  MON_UID=$(id -u "$MON_USER")
  MON_GID=$(id -g "$MON_USER")
}

# env_set FILE KEY VALUE: set KEY=VALUE in a root-only env file.
env_set() {
  local f="$1" k="$2" v="$3"
  touch "$f"
  chmod 600 "$f"
  if grep -q "^${k}=" "$f"; then
    sed -i "s|^${k}=.*|${k}=${v}|" "$f"
  else
    printf '%s=%s\n' "$k" "$v" >> "$f"
  fi
}

# stack_ids DIR [logging]: write the ids the compose file needs into DIR/.env.
stack_ids() {
  local dir="$1" docker_gid journal_gid
  mon_ids
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_UID "$MON_UID"
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_GID "$MON_GID"
  if [[ "${2:-}" == logging ]]; then
    docker_gid=$(getent group docker | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$docker_gid" ]] || die "docker group missing"
    env_set "${dir}/.env" DOCKER_GID "$docker_gid"
    journal_gid=$(getent group systemd-journal | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$journal_gid" ]] || die "systemd-journal group missing"
    # compose rejects equal group_add items, so the journal group is only added when it differs from docker's.
    if [[ "$journal_gid" != "$docker_gid" ]]; then
      printf 'services:\n  alloy:\n    group_add:\n      - "%s"\n' "$journal_gid" > "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    else
      rm -f "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    fi
  fi
}

# Config readable by the service user, writable by root only; .env (secrets) stays root-only.
own_stack_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  chown -R "root:${MON_USER}" "$dir"
  chmod -R u=rwX,g=rX,o= "$dir"
  if [[ -f "${dir}/.env" ]]; then
    chown root:root "${dir}/.env"
    chmod 600 "${dir}/.env"
  fi
}

# Create the stack's containers and volumes without starting them, then hand the volumes to the service user.
chown_volumes() {
  local dir="$1" project v
  mon_ids
  project=$(cd "$dir" && docker compose config --format json | jq -r .name)
  (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start --quiet-pull >/dev/null 2>&1) || (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start >/dev/null)
  for v in $(docker volume ls -q --filter "label=com.docker.compose.project=${project}"); do
    docker run --rm --network none -v "${v}:/v" alpine:3.20 chown -R "${MON_UID}:${MON_GID}" /v
  done
}
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/lib.sh"

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/admin-tools.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Admin tools for the runner host: htop, btop, ncdu, iotop, mtr, network and disk tools, editors, skopeo, mc, fd, rg, fzf.
# Each package is installed on its own, so one that is missing on this release doesn't stop the rest.
# Also installs the shell aliases, shell history and fzf key bindings (Ctrl+R, Ctrl+T) and, if there is none yet, the tmux config from the init. Safe to rerun.
set -Eeuo pipefail
# shellcheck source=lib.sh
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"

if [[ "${1:-}" == "-h" || "${1:-}" == "--help" ]]; then
  echo "Usage: $0  installs admin tools (htop, btop, ncdu, iotop, mtr, dig, tcpdump, skopeo, mc, fd, rg, fzf, ...), aliases, shell history and tmux config"
  exit 0
fi
require_root

if command -v dnf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  dnf -y -q install epel-release >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: epel-release not available, EPEL tools will be missing"
  PKGS=(htop btop ncdu iotop mtr nano vim-enhanced rsync wget unzip tar bash-completion skopeo dnf-utils
        bind-utils lsof sysstat tcpdump traceroute nmap-ncat jq git tmux curl mc fd-find ripgrep fzf)
  pkg_one() { dnf -y -q install "$1" >/dev/null 2>&1; }
elif command -v apt-get >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  apt-get update -q >/dev/null
  PKGS=(htop btop ncdu iotop mtr-tiny nano vim rsync wget unzip tar bash-completion skopeo
        dnsutils lsof sysstat tcpdump traceroute netcat-openbsd jq git tmux curl mc fd-find ripgrep fzf)
  pkg_one() { DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get install -y -q "$1" >/dev/null 2>&1; }
else
  die "no supported package manager"
fi

installed=()
missing=()
for p in "${PKGS[@]}"; do
  if pkg_one "$p"; then
    installed+=("$p")
  else
    missing+=("$p")
  fi
done

if [[ -f /etc/default/sysstat ]]; then
  sed -i 's/^ENABLED=.*/ENABLED="true"/' /etc/default/sysstat
fi
if systemctl cat sysstat >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  systemctl enable --now sysstat >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: could not enable sysstat collection"
fi

cat > /etc/profile.d/ci-runner.sh <<'EOF'
alias health='sudo /usr/local/bin/health'
alias runner-logs='sudo journalctl -u gitlab-runner -f'
alias runner-errors='sudo journalctl -u gitlab-runner --since "-30min" --no-pager | grep -Ei "error|warn|bottleneck"'
alias runner-jobs='sudo docker ps --filter label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true --format "{{.Names}}  {{.Status}}"'
alias runner-metrics='curl -s 127.0.0.1:9252/metrics | grep "^gitlab_runner_"'
alias dfd='df -h / ; sudo docker system df'
alias dstats='sudo docker stats --no-stream'
EOF
chmod 644 /etc/profile.d/ci-runner.sh
restorecon_if /etc/profile.d/ci-runner.sh

# Shell history, Ctrl+R / Ctrl+T with fzf: one source file, also embedded in server/server-init-*.sh
install -m 0644 "$REPO_ROOT/common/shell-history.sh" /etc/profile.d/90-shell-history.sh
restorecon_if /etc/profile.d/90-shell-history.sh
# Debian and Ubuntu ship HISTSIZE=1000/HISTFILESIZE=2000 in the skeleton ~/.bashrc; bash applies them before the
# first prompt and truncates the history file, so the larger limits in profile.d never take effect. Comment out
# exactly those default lines (other values are a deliberate choice and are kept).
for rc in /etc/skel/.bashrc /root/.bashrc /home/*/.bashrc; do
  [[ -f "$rc" ]] && sed -i -E 's/^(HISTSIZE=1000|HISTFILESIZE=2000)$/# &  (raised in \/etc\/profile.d\/90-shell-history.sh)/' "$rc"
done
# Debian and Ubuntu name the fd binary fdfind
if command -v fdfind >/dev/null 2>&1 && ! command -v fd >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  ln -sfn "$(command -v fdfind)" /usr/local/bin/fd
fi

# root and every admin in the ssh-users group (runner/install/users.sh) get the tmux config if they have none
homes=(/root)
while IFS= read -r u; do
  [[ -n "$u" ]] && homes+=("$(getent passwd "$u" | cut -d: -f6)")
done < <(getent group ssh-users | cut -d: -f4 | tr ',' '\n')
for home in "${homes[@]}"; do
  [[ -d "$home" ]] || continue
  if [[ -f "${home}/.tmux.conf" ]]; then
    log "kept existing ${home}/.tmux.conf"
    continue
  fi
  cat > "${home}/.tmux.conf" <<'EOF'
set -g mouse on
set -g history-limit 50000
set -g base-index 1
set -g status-right '#H  %H:%M'
EOF
  chown "$(stat -c '%U:%G' "$home")" "${home}/.tmux.conf"
  chmod 644 "${home}/.tmux.conf"
done

log "installed or already present (${#installed[@]}): ${installed[*]}"
if (( ${#missing[@]} )); then
  log "WARN: not available on this release (${#missing[@]}): ${missing[*]}"
fi
for c in htop btop ncdu iotop mtr dig tcpdump skopeo sar mc fd rg fzf; do
  if command -v "$c" >/dev/null 2>&1; then printf '  %-8s ok\n' "$c"; else printf '  %-8s missing\n' "$c"; fi
done
log "OK: aliases work in new shells (or run: source /etc/profile.d/ci-runner.sh); Docker and journal aliases use sudo"
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/admin-tools.sh"

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/shell-history.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
# shellcheck shell=bash
# Shell history and fzf key bindings for interactive bash (Ctrl+R history search, Ctrl+T file search).
# Installed as /etc/profile.d/90-shell-history.sh (runner hosts) or 90-server-shell.sh (plain servers).
# The same text is embedded in the server/server-init-*.sh scripts (they must stay self-contained):
# keep all copies identical, tools/test-cloud-init.py checks that.
[ -n "${BASH_VERSION-}" ] || return 0
[[ $- == *i* ]] || return 0

__shell_history_vars() {
  HISTSIZE=100000
  HISTFILESIZE=200000
  HISTTIMEFORMAT='%F %T  '
  HISTCONTROL=ignoreboth:erasedups
}
__shell_history_vars
shopt -s histappend cmdhist

# Run before each prompt: append the last command to the history file so parallel sessions share history.
# On Debian and Ubuntu the user's ~/.bashrc runs after this file and lowers the limits, so the first
# prompt applies them again (a later change by hand in a session is kept).
__shell_history_prompt() {
  history -a
  if [[ -z "${__shell_history_done-}" ]]; then
    __shell_history_done=1
    __shell_history_vars
  fi
}
# Keeps an existing PROMPT_COMMAND (string or array) and is safe to source twice.
# When PROMPT_COMMAND is still empty (Fedora/EL login shells read profile.d before /etc/bashrc, which sets
# the terminal title only if PROMPT_COMMAND is empty), use an array with an empty first element: /etc/bashrc
# then fills element 0 with its title command and the hook stays in element 1 (bash 5.1+ runs every element).
if [[ -z "${PROMPT_COMMAND-}" && "$(declare -p PROMPT_COMMAND 2>/dev/null)" != "declare -a"* ]] && (( BASH_VERSINFO[0] > 5 || (BASH_VERSINFO[0] == 5 && BASH_VERSINFO[1] >= 1) )); then
  unset PROMPT_COMMAND
  PROMPT_COMMAND=('' '__shell_history_prompt')
fi
case "$(declare -p PROMPT_COMMAND 2>/dev/null)" in
  "declare -a"*)
    [[ " ${PROMPT_COMMAND[*]} " == *" __shell_history_prompt "* ]] || PROMPT_COMMAND+=('__shell_history_prompt')
    ;;
  *)
    if [[ "${PROMPT_COMMAND-}" != *__shell_history_prompt* ]]; then
      __pc="${PROMPT_COMMAND-}"
      while [[ "$__pc" == *[[:space:]\;] ]]; do __pc="${__pc%?}"; done
      # shellcheck disable=SC2178
      PROMPT_COMMAND="${__pc:+$__pc;}__shell_history_prompt"
      unset __pc
    fi
    ;;
esac

# Debian and Ubuntu install fd as fdfind.
if ! command -v fd >/dev/null 2>&1 && command -v fdfind >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  alias fd=fdfind
  __fd=fdfind
elif command -v fd >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  __fd=fd
fi
if [[ -n "${__fd-}" ]]; then
  export FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND="${FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND:-$__fd --type f --hidden --follow --exclude .git}"
  export FZF_CTRL_T_COMMAND="${FZF_CTRL_T_COMMAND:-$FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND}"
  export FZF_ALT_C_COMMAND="${FZF_ALT_C_COMMAND:-$__fd --type d --hidden --follow --exclude .git}"
fi
unset __fd

if command -v fzf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  for __f in /usr/share/fzf/shell/key-bindings.bash /usr/share/doc/fzf/examples/key-bindings.bash; do
    if [[ -r "$__f" ]]; then
      # shellcheck source=/dev/null
      . "$__f"
      __fzf_done=1
      break
    fi
  done
  # fzf 0.48+ can print its own bindings when the package ships no file.
  if [[ -z "${__fzf_done-}" ]] && __fzf_b=$(fzf --bash 2>/dev/null); then
    eval "$__fzf_b"
  fi
  unset __f __fzf_done __fzf_b
fi
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/shell-history.sh"

echo "files written to $R: 3"
bash "$R/common/admin-tools.sh" </dev/null
INSTALL
````

<a id="tools"></a>
## Health, cleanup, job isolation, engine updates

Installs `/usr/local/bin/health` and the cleanup: weekly run on Monday 03:30 (Docker images, build cache, runner caches, old builds, packages, journal, temp files) plus the disk guard, which runs an emergency cleanup every 30 minutes when the disk is 85% full. Also the job isolation (CI jobs cannot reach the cloud metadata address or services on the host) and the Sunday 04:30 upgrade of Docker CE and GitLab Runner, which runs only while no job runs. Also the kernel hardening (sysctl) and no core dumps. Ends with a dry run of the cleanup.

````bash
sudo bash <<'INSTALL'
set -Eeuo pipefail
R=/root/server-init-config

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/lib.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Shared helpers for the installers (runner/install, server and common). Sourced, not executed.
# shellcheck shell=bash

REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
export REPO_ROOT

log() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

require_root() { [[ $EUID -eq 0 ]] || die "run as root"; }

selinux_on() { command -v selinuxenabled >/dev/null 2>&1 && selinuxenabled; }

pkg_install() {
  if command -v dnf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    dnf -y -q install "$@"
  elif command -v apt-get >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    apt-get update -q && apt-get install -y -q "$@"
  else
    die "no supported package manager"
  fi
}

# Label a directory so containers may read files in it (no-op without SELinux).
selinux_label_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  install -d -m 0755 "$dir"
  selinux_on || return 0
  command -v semanage >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_install policycoreutils-python-utils
  # Read the whole list first: grep -q exits at the first match and would SIGPIPE the producer under pipefail.
  if ! grep -q "^${dir}(/\.\*)?" <<<"$(semanage fcontext -l)"; then
    semanage fcontext -a -t container_file_t "${dir}(/.*)?"
  fi
  restorecon -R "$dir"
}

restorecon_if() { if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon "$@" || true; fi; }

# RHEL-family sudo resets PATH to secure_path, which can leave out /usr/local/bin (so `sudo health` fails).
# Adds a validated drop-in unless some sudoers file already sets a secure_path that includes it.
ensure_secure_path() {
  local f=/etc/sudoers.d/50-secure-path tmp
  command -v visudo >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  if grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path[[:space:]]*=.*/usr/local/bin' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0
  fi
  if ! grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0   # no secure_path at all (Debian family keeps it in sudoers; others use the caller PATH)
  fi
  tmp=$(mktemp)
  printf 'Defaults secure_path="/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"\n' > "$tmp"
  if visudo -cqf "$tmp"; then
    install -d -m 0750 /etc/sudoers.d
    install -m 0440 -o root -g root "$tmp" "$f"
    log "sudo secure_path now includes /usr/local/bin (${f})"
  else
    echo "WARN: generated sudoers drop-in is invalid, secure_path left as it was" >&2
  fi
  rm -f "$tmp"
}

ci_jobs_running() { docker ps -q --filter "label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true" 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }

require_runner_idle() {
  local n
  n=$(ci_jobs_running)
  (( n == 0 )) || die "${n} CI job container(s) running, retry when the runner is idle"
}

# Timestamped copy before a change. Skipped when an identical backup already exists, so reruns of an idempotent
# installer do not pile up copies of an unchanged file.
backup() {
  local f=$1 b
  for b in "${f}".bak.*; do
    [[ -f "$b" ]] && cmp -s "$f" "$b" && return 0
  done
  cp -a "$f" "${f}.bak.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
}

# Prometheus scrape jobs for the logging stack, shared by runner/install/logging.sh and runner/install/update.sh.
prom_jobs_logging() {
  cat <<'YAML'
  - job_name: loki
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:3100"]
  - job_name: alloy
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:12345"]
YAML
}

# Merge KEY=VALUE entries into the single [[runners]] environment line of config.toml.
runner_env_merge() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml existing list key e line
  [[ $(grep -c '^\[\[runners\]\]' "$cfg") -eq 1 ]] || die "expected exactly one [[runners]] block in $cfg"
  existing=$(grep -E '^  environment = \[' "$cfg" | sed -E 's/^  environment = \[(.*)\]$/\1/' || true)
  list="$existing"
  for e in "$@"; do
    key="${e%%=*}"
    if grep -q "\"${key}=" <<<"$list"; then
      list=$(sed -E "s|\"${key}=[^\"]*\"|\"${e}\"|" <<<"$list")
    else
      list="${list:+${list}, }\"${e}\""
    fi
  done
  line="  environment = [${list}]"
  if grep -qE '^  environment = \[' "$cfg"; then
    sed -i -E "s|^  environment = \[.*\]$|${line}|" "$cfg"
  else
    sed -i -E "0,/^  executor = .*/s||${line}\n&|" "$cfg"
  fi
  chmod 600 "$cfg"
  runner_cfg_owner
}

# When the runner service runs as gitlab-runner (runner/install/users.sh), it must own its config after root edits it.
runner_cfg_owner() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
  if [[ -f /etc/systemd/system/gitlab-runner.service.d/10-service-user.conf ]] && id gitlab-runner >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    chown -R gitlab-runner:gitlab-runner /etc/gitlab-runner
    chmod 600 "$cfg"
  fi
}

# --- service user for the monitoring and logging stacks (created by runner/install/users.sh) ---------------
MON_USER="${MON_USER:-monitoring}"

# Set MON_UID and MON_GID, or stop if the service user is missing.
mon_ids() {
  id "$MON_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "service user ${MON_USER} missing, run runner/install/users.sh first"
  MON_UID=$(id -u "$MON_USER")
  MON_GID=$(id -g "$MON_USER")
}

# env_set FILE KEY VALUE: set KEY=VALUE in a root-only env file.
env_set() {
  local f="$1" k="$2" v="$3"
  touch "$f"
  chmod 600 "$f"
  if grep -q "^${k}=" "$f"; then
    sed -i "s|^${k}=.*|${k}=${v}|" "$f"
  else
    printf '%s=%s\n' "$k" "$v" >> "$f"
  fi
}

# stack_ids DIR [logging]: write the ids the compose file needs into DIR/.env.
stack_ids() {
  local dir="$1" docker_gid journal_gid
  mon_ids
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_UID "$MON_UID"
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_GID "$MON_GID"
  if [[ "${2:-}" == logging ]]; then
    docker_gid=$(getent group docker | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$docker_gid" ]] || die "docker group missing"
    env_set "${dir}/.env" DOCKER_GID "$docker_gid"
    journal_gid=$(getent group systemd-journal | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$journal_gid" ]] || die "systemd-journal group missing"
    # compose rejects equal group_add items, so the journal group is only added when it differs from docker's.
    if [[ "$journal_gid" != "$docker_gid" ]]; then
      printf 'services:\n  alloy:\n    group_add:\n      - "%s"\n' "$journal_gid" > "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    else
      rm -f "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    fi
  fi
}

# Config readable by the service user, writable by root only; .env (secrets) stays root-only.
own_stack_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  chown -R "root:${MON_USER}" "$dir"
  chmod -R u=rwX,g=rX,o= "$dir"
  if [[ -f "${dir}/.env" ]]; then
    chown root:root "${dir}/.env"
    chmod 600 "${dir}/.env"
  fi
}

# Create the stack's containers and volumes without starting them, then hand the volumes to the service user.
chown_volumes() {
  local dir="$1" project v
  mon_ids
  project=$(cd "$dir" && docker compose config --format json | jq -r .name)
  (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start --quiet-pull >/dev/null 2>&1) || (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start >/dev/null)
  for v in $(docker volume ls -q --filter "label=com.docker.compose.project=${project}"); do
    docker run --rm --network none -v "${v}:/v" alpine:3.20 chown -R "${MON_UID}:${MON_GID}" /v
  done
}
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/lib.sh"

install -d "$R/runner/install"
cat > "$R/runner/install/host-tools.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Install health (plus the sudo secure_path fix so `sudo health` works), cleanup and disk guard scripts plus their systemd timers,
# the job isolation rules (CI jobs cannot reach the metadata service or host services), the weekly Docker/GitLab Runner update,
# and the kernel hardening (sysctl) and core dump settings that the cloud-init files also write.
# Use on servers built without the cloud-init in this repo, or to update the scripts.
set -Eeuo pipefail
# shellcheck source=../../common/lib.sh
source "$(dirname "$0")/../../common/lib.sh"

if [[ "${1:-}" == "-h" || "${1:-}" == "--help" ]]; then
  echo "Usage: $0  installs common/health, cleanup, disk guard and systemd timers"
  exit 0
fi
require_root

install -m 0755 "$REPO_ROOT/common/health" /usr/local/bin/health
restorecon_if /usr/local/bin/health
for f in runner-weekly-cleanup.sh runner-disk-guard.sh runner-engine-update.sh ci-job-isolation.sh; do
  install -m 0755 "$REPO_ROOT/runner/scripts/$f" "/usr/local/bin/$f"
  restorecon_if "/usr/local/bin/$f"
done
ensure_secure_path

for u in runner-weekly-cleanup.service runner-weekly-cleanup.timer runner-disk-guard.service runner-disk-guard.timer runner-engine-update.service runner-engine-update.timer ci-job-isolation.service; do
  install -m 0644 "$REPO_ROOT/runner/systemd/$u" "/etc/systemd/system/$u"
  restorecon_if "/etc/systemd/system/$u"
done

# Kernel hardening and no core dumps: the same files and contents as the cloud-init files (keys the kernel lacks are ignored).
install -D -m 0644 "$REPO_ROOT/common/sysctl-hardening.conf" /etc/sysctl.d/90-ci-hardening.conf
install -D -m 0644 /dev/stdin /etc/security/limits.d/50-nocore.conf <<'LIMITS'
* hard core 0
LIMITS
install -D -m 0644 /dev/stdin /etc/systemd/coredump.conf.d/50-off.conf <<'COREDUMP'
[Coredump]
Storage=none
ProcessSizeMax=0
COREDUMP
restorecon_if /etc/sysctl.d/90-ci-hardening.conf
sysctl --system >/dev/null || log "WARN: sysctl --system reported an error"

systemctl daemon-reload
systemctl enable --now runner-weekly-cleanup.timer runner-disk-guard.timer runner-engine-update.timer
# Needs Docker (the DOCKER-USER chain). On a host without it the unit is enabled and applies at the next Docker start.
if systemctl is-active --quiet docker; then systemctl enable --now ci-job-isolation.service; else systemctl enable ci-job-isolation.service; fi

DRY_RUN=1 /usr/local/bin/runner-weekly-cleanup.sh >/dev/null
systemctl list-timers 'runner-*' --no-pager
log "OK: run 'health' for a full report (it uses sudo itself when needed)"
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/runner/install/host-tools.sh"

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/health" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# health: one-command health and status report for this host (GitLab Runner host or general server)
set -Eeuo pipefail

DISK_WARN=70
DISK_FAIL=85
INODE_WARN=80
MEM_WARN=15
MEM_FAIL=5
LOAD_WARN=1.5
LOAD_FAIL=3
CLAM_SIG_WARN_DAYS=7
QUICK=0
BRIEF=0
WRITE=0
ONLY_CONTAINERS=0
COLOR=1
PROFILE=auto
RUNNER_UNREGISTERED=0
CFG=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
PROM_URL="${PROM_URL:-http://127.0.0.1:9090}"
FORECAST_MIN_HISTORY_H="${FORECAST_MIN_HISTORY_H:-6}"

usage() {
  cat <<'USAGE'
Usage: health [options]

One-command health and status report for this host. Works on a GitLab Runner
host and on a general server.

Run it as a normal user: it re-runs itself through sudo when that needs no password.
Without root and without passwordless sudo it still runs, but skips the root-only
checks (firewall rules, fail2ban, sshd settings, journal, docker, runner).

Options:
  -q, --quick       skip slow checks (runner verify, network, pending updates, needs-restarting)
  -c, --containers  only show health of running containers
  --profile P       server|runner (default: auto = server; runner only when a runner is registered)
  --brief           compact summary (7 lines) of the state the server timers cached, plus live disk, memory,
                    firewall and fail2ban; this is what the admin sees on login
  --write           with --brief, as root: save it to /var/lib/server-health/brief.txt (done by server-health-brief.timer;
                    also runs a full report at most every 6 hours and notifies when it has failures)
  --no-color        plain output without colors
  -h, --help        show this help

Exit codes: 0 all OK, 1 warnings only, 2 at least one failure
USAGE
}

ORIG_ARGS=("$@")
while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do
  case "$1" in
    -q|--quick) QUICK=1 ;;
    -c|--containers) ONLY_CONTAINERS=1 ;;
    --profile) [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || { echo "--profile needs a value: runner or server" >&2; exit 2; }; shift; PROFILE="$1" ;;
    --profile=*) PROFILE="${1#--profile=}" ;;
    --no-color) COLOR=0 ;;
    --brief) BRIEF=1 ;;
    --write) WRITE=1 ;;
    -h|--help) usage; exit 0 ;;
    *) echo "unknown option: $1" >&2; usage >&2; exit 2 ;;
  esac
  shift
done

if (( WRITE == 1 && BRIEF == 0 )); then echo "--write needs --brief" >&2; exit 2; fi
case "$PROFILE" in
  auto|runner|server) ;;
  *) echo "invalid --profile '${PROFILE}': use runner or server" >&2; exit 2 ;;
esac

LIMITED=0
# Not root: re-run through sudo when that works without a password (HEALTH_REEXEC stops loops);
# otherwise run in limited mode and skip the checks that need root.
if [[ $EUID -ne 0 ]]; then
  if [[ -z "${HEALTH_REEXEC:-}" ]] && command -v sudo >/dev/null 2>&1 && sudo -n true >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    exec sudo -n -- env HEALTH_REEXEC=1 "$(readlink -f "$0")" "${ORIG_ARGS[@]}"
  fi
  LIMITED=1
fi

if [[ -t 1 && "$COLOR" == "1" && -z "${NO_COLOR:-}" ]]; then
  C_OK=$'\e[32m'; C_WARN=$'\e[33m'; C_FAIL=$'\e[31m'; C_B=$'\e[1m'; C_D=$'\e[2m'; C_N=$'\e[0m'
else
  C_OK=""; C_WARN=""; C_FAIL=""; C_B=""; C_D=""; C_N=""
fi

N_OK=0; N_WARN=0; N_FAIL=0
FW_STATE=unknown
ok()      { N_OK=$((N_OK + 1));     printf '  %s%-5s%s %s\n' "$C_OK" "OK" "$C_N" "$*"; }
warn()    { N_WARN=$((N_WARN + 1)); printf '  %s%-5s%s %s\n' "$C_WARN" "WARN" "$C_N" "$*"; }
fail()    { N_FAIL=$((N_FAIL + 1)); printf '  %s%-5s%s %s\n' "$C_FAIL" "FAIL" "$C_N" "$*"; }
skip()    { printf '  %s%-5s %s%s\n' "$C_D" "--" "$*" "$C_N"; }
info()    { printf '  %s%-5s %s%s\n' "$C_D" "" "$*" "$C_N"; }
section() { printf '\n%s%s%s\n' "$C_B" "$*" "$C_N"; }
have()    { command -v "$1" >/dev/null 2>&1; }
gt()      { awk -v a="$1" -v b="$2" 'BEGIN { exit !(a > b) }'; }
isnum()   { [[ "${1:-}" =~ ^-?[0-9]+([.][0-9]+)?([eE][-+]?[0-9]+)?$ ]]; }
num()     { awk -v x="$1" -v d="${2:-1}" 'BEGIN { printf "%.*f", d, x }'; }
unit_exists() { systemctl list-unit-files "$1" --no-legend 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; }
is_runner()   { [[ "$PROFILE" == "runner" ]]; }
detect_profile() {
  [[ "$PROFILE" == "auto" ]] || return 0
  # Default is a plain server. Only a host with a registered runner gets the runner checks;
  # gitlab-runner installed but never registered is still a plain server.
  if [[ -r "$CFG" ]] && grep -q '^[[:space:]]*\[\[runners\]\]' "$CFG" 2>/dev/null; then
    PROFILE=runner
  else
    PROFILE=server
    # Without root the 0600 config.toml can't be read, so "not registered" would be a guess.
    if [[ -e "$CFG" && ! -r "$CFG" ]]; then RUNNER_UNREGISTERED=2
    elif have gitlab-runner || unit_exists gitlab-runner.service; then RUNNER_UNREGISTERED=1; fi
  fi
}
http_code()   { curl -s -o /dev/null -m "${2:-5}" -w '%{http_code}' "$1" 2>/dev/null || true; }

human_age() {
  local s="$1"
  if (( s < 3600 )); then echo "$(( s / 60 ))m"
  elif (( s < 86400 )); then echo "$(( s / 3600 ))h$(( s % 3600 / 60 ))m"
  else echo "$(( s / 86400 ))d$(( s % 86400 / 3600 ))h"; fi
}

prom_q() {
  curl -fsS -m 10 -G "${PROM_URL}/api/v1/query" --data-urlencode "query=$1" 2>/dev/null \
    | jq -r '.data.result[0].value[1] // empty' 2>/dev/null || true
}

prom_qv() {
  curl -fsS -m 10 -G "${PROM_URL}/api/v1/query" --data-urlencode "query=$1" 2>/dev/null \
    | jq -r '.data.result[] | ((.metric | to_entries | map(.value) | join(",")) as $k | "\($k)=\(.value[1])")' 2>/dev/null || true
}

print_summary_and_exit() {
  section "Summary"
  printf '  %s%d OK%s  %s%d WARN%s  %s%d FAIL%s\n' \
    "$C_OK" "$N_OK" "$C_N" "$C_WARN" "$N_WARN" "$C_N" "$C_FAIL" "$N_FAIL" "$C_N"
  if (( N_FAIL > 0 )); then exit 2; fi
  if (( N_WARN > 0 )); then exit 1; fi
  exit 0
}

check_system() {
  section "System"
  local pretty="unknown"
  if [[ -r /etc/os-release ]]; then
    pretty=$(awk -F= '/^PRETTY_NAME=/ { gsub(/"/, "", $2); print $2 }' /etc/os-release)
  fi
  info "$(uname -n) | ${pretty} | $(uname -m) | kernel $(uname -r)"
  info "uptime $(uptime -p 2>/dev/null | sed 's/^up //') | $(nproc) vCPU"

  local cores load5 per_core
  cores=$(nproc)
  read -r _ load5 _ _ _ < /proc/loadavg
  per_core=$(awk -v l="$load5" -v c="$cores" 'BEGIN { printf "%.2f", l / c }')
  if gt "$per_core" "$LOAD_FAIL"; then
    fail "load ${load5} (5 min), ${per_core} per core"
  elif gt "$per_core" "$LOAD_WARN"; then
    warn "load ${load5} (5 min), ${per_core} per core"
  else
    ok "load ${load5} (5 min), ${per_core} per core"
  fi

  local mem_total mem_avail mem_pct msg
  mem_total=$(awk '/^MemTotal:/ { print $2 }' /proc/meminfo)
  mem_avail=$(awk '/^MemAvailable:/ { print $2 }' /proc/meminfo)
  mem_pct=$(( mem_avail * 100 / mem_total ))
  msg="memory $(( (mem_total - mem_avail) / 1024 ))/$(( mem_total / 1024 )) MB used, ${mem_pct}% available"
  if (( mem_pct < MEM_FAIL )); then fail "$msg"
  elif (( mem_pct < MEM_WARN )); then warn "$msg"
  else ok "$msg"; fi

  local swap_total swap_free
  swap_total=$(awk '/^SwapTotal:/ { print $2 }' /proc/meminfo)
  swap_free=$(awk '/^SwapFree:/ { print $2 }' /proc/meminfo)
  if (( swap_total > 0 )); then
    local swap_pct=$(( (swap_total - swap_free) * 100 / swap_total ))
    msg="swap $(( (swap_total - swap_free) / 1024 ))/$(( swap_total / 1024 )) MB used (${swap_pct}%)"
    if is_runner; then info "$msg"
    elif (( swap_pct >= 80 )); then warn "$msg, memory pressure"
    else ok "$msg"; fi
  else
    info "swap: none"
  fi

  local kills ooms victims
  # Count kills, not every related kernel line. One kill emits several matches.
  if (( LIMITED )); then
    kills=""
    skip "OOM kills: needs root (kernel log)"
  else
    kills=$(journalctl -k -b --no-pager -o cat 2>/dev/null | grep 'Out of memory: Killed process' || true)
  fi
  if [[ -n "$kills" ]]; then
    ooms=$(grep -c . <<<"$kills")
    victims=$(sed -nE 's/.*Killed process [0-9]+ \(([^)]+)\).*/\1/p' <<<"$kills" | sort | uniq -c | awk '{printf "%s x%s, ", $2, $1}' | sed 's/, $//')
    warn "${ooms} OOM kill(s) since boot${victims:+ (${victims})} (journalctl -k -b | grep 'Killed process')"
  elif (( ! LIMITED )); then
    ok "no OOM kills since boot"
  fi

  local disk_pct disk_info inode_pct
  disk_pct=$(df --output=pcent / | tail -1 | tr -dc '0-9')
  disk_info=$(df -h --output=used,size / | tail -1 | awk '{ print $1 "/" $2 }')
  msg="disk / ${disk_pct}% used (${disk_info})"
  if (( disk_pct >= DISK_FAIL )); then fail "$msg"
  elif (( disk_pct >= DISK_WARN )); then warn "$msg"
  else ok "$msg"; fi

  inode_pct=$(df --output=ipcent / 2>/dev/null | tail -1 | tr -dc '0-9' || true)
  if (( ${inode_pct:-0} >= INODE_WARN )); then
    warn "inodes / ${inode_pct}% used"
  fi

  local failed
  failed=$(systemctl --failed --no-legend --plain 2>/dev/null | awk '{ print $1 }' | tr '\n' ' ' || true)
  if [[ -n "${failed// /}" ]]; then
    fail "failed units: ${failed}"
  else
    ok "no failed systemd units"
  fi

  if have timedatectl; then
    local sync
    sync=$(timedatectl show -p NTPSynchronized --value 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [[ "$sync" == "yes" ]]; then ok "clock synchronized (NTP)"
    else warn "clock not synchronized (timedatectl)"; fi
  fi

  if have cloud-init; then
    local ci
    ci=$(cloud-init status 2>/dev/null | awk -F': ' '/^status:/ { print $2 }' || true)
    case "$ci" in
      done) ok "cloud-init done" ;;
      running) warn "cloud-init still running" ;;
      "") skip "cloud-init status unknown" ;;
      *) warn "cloud-init status: ${ci}" ;;
    esac
  fi
  return 0
}

check_security() {
  section "Security"
  if have getenforce; then
    local mode denials raw rc=0
    mode=$(getenforce 2>/dev/null || true)
    case "$mode" in
      Enforcing) ok "SELinux enforcing" ;;
      Permissive) warn "SELinux permissive (logs only, does not block)" ;;
      *) warn "SELinux ${mode:-unknown}" ;;
    esac
    # ausearch treats a pipe on stdin as the audit log and blocks until it closes.
    # health is often started over ssh, so force the on-disk logs and bound the query.
    if (( LIMITED )); then
      skip "SELinux denials: needs root"
    elif ! have ausearch; then
      skip "ausearch not installed"
    else
      raw=$(timeout 10 ausearch --input-logs -m avc -ts today </dev/null 2>/dev/null) || rc=$?
      if (( rc == 124 )); then
        skip "SELinux denial query timed out"
      elif (( rc != 0 && rc != 1 )); then
        skip "could not query SELinux denials (ausearch exit ${rc})"
      else
        denials=$(grep -c 'avc:' <<<"$raw" || true)
        if (( ${denials:-0} > 0 )); then
          warn "${denials} SELinux denials today (ausearch --input-logs -m avc -ts today)"
        else
          ok "no SELinux denials today"
        fi
      fi
    fi
  fi

  if (( LIMITED )); then
    skip "sshd settings, listening ports, pending updates: need root (run as root for the full report)"
  elif have sshd; then
    local sshd_t root_login
    sshd_t=$(sshd -T 2>/dev/null || true)
    if grep -qx 'passwordauthentication no' <<<"$sshd_t"; then
      ok "SSH password login disabled"
    elif is_runner; then
      fail "SSH password login enabled"
    else
      warn "SSH password login enabled (set PasswordAuthentication no)"
    fi
    root_login=$(awk '/^permitrootlogin / { print $2 }' <<<"$sshd_t")
    if is_runner; then
      case "$root_login" in
        no)  ok "SSH root login disabled" ;;
        yes) fail "SSH root login with password allowed" ;;
        *)   warn "SSH root login by key still allowed (${root_login:-unknown}); disable: sudo bash -c \"cd /root/server-init-config && runner/setup.sh --lock-root\"" ;;
      esac
      if grep -q '^allowgroups ' <<<"$sshd_t"; then
        ok "SSH limited to groups: $(awk '/^allowgroups / { printf "%s ", $2 }' <<<"$sshd_t")"
      else
        warn "SSH not limited by AllowGroups (runner/install/users.sh sets it)"
      fi
    else
      case "$root_login" in
        yes) warn "SSH root login with password allowed (PermitRootLogin yes)" ;;
        *)   info "SSH root login: ${root_login:-unknown}" ;;
      esac
    fi
  fi

  local members u shell pid ru
  members=$(getent group docker 2>/dev/null | cut -d: -f4 | tr ',' '\n' | grep -vx 'gitlab-runner' | grep . | tr '\n' ' ' || true)
  if [[ -n "${members// /}" ]]; then
    warn "users in the docker group (root-equivalent): ${members}; use sudo docker instead"
  else
    if is_runner; then ok "docker group: only gitlab-runner"; else ok "docker group: no members"; fi
  fi
  for u in gitlab-runner monitoring; do
    id "$u" >/dev/null 2>&1 || continue
    shell=$(getent passwd "$u" | cut -d: -f7)
    case "$shell" in
      */nologin|*/false) ok "service user ${u}: no login shell" ;;
      *) warn "service user ${u} has login shell ${shell}" ;;
    esac
  done
  if have systemctl && systemctl is-active --quiet gitlab-runner 2>/dev/null; then
    pid=$(systemctl show -p MainPID --value gitlab-runner 2>/dev/null || true)
    ru=$(ps -o user= -p "${pid:-0}" 2>/dev/null | tr -d ' ' || true)
    if [[ "$ru" == root ]]; then
      warn "gitlab-runner service runs as root (runner/install/users.sh switches it to gitlab-runner)"
    elif [[ -n "$ru" ]]; then
      ok "gitlab-runner service runs as ${ru}"
    fi
  fi

  local exposed ssh_ports allowed
  if (( LIMITED == 0 )); then
    ssh_ports=$(sshd -T 2>/dev/null | awk '/^port / { print $2 }' | sort -un | tr '\n' ' ' || true)
    ssh_ports="${ssh_ports:-22 }"
    allowed="$(tr ' ' '|' <<<"${ssh_ports% }")|80|443"
    if ! is_runner; then
      info "SSH port: ${ssh_ports% }"
    elif [[ " ${ssh_ports}" == *" 22 "* ]]; then
      info "SSH listens on: ${ssh_ports}(port 22 closes with the lock-root step)"
    else
      ok "SSH port: ${ssh_ports% }"
    fi
    exposed=$(ss -ltnH 2>/dev/null | awk '{ print $4 }' \
      | grep -vE '^(127\.|\[::1\]|172\.17\.)' \
      | sed -E 's/.*:([0-9]+)$/\1/' | sort -un | grep -vxE "$allowed" | tr '\n' ' ' || true)
    if [[ -n "${exposed// /}" ]]; then
      if is_runner; then
        warn "ports listening on public interfaces: ${exposed}(make sure the Hetzner firewall blocks them)"
      elif [[ "$FW_STATE" == "active" ]]; then
        info "ports listening on public interfaces: ${exposed}(the host firewall decides what is reachable)"
      else
        warn "ports listening on public interfaces: ${exposed}(no active host firewall)"
      fi
    else
      ok "only SSH/HTTP/HTTPS listen on public interfaces"
    fi

  fi

  if (( QUICK == 0 && LIMITED == 0 )); then
    local out n
    if have dnf; then
      if out=$(timeout 90 dnf -q updateinfo list --security 2>/dev/null); then
        n=$(grep -c . <<<"$out" || true)
        if (( ${n:-0} > 0 )); then
          warn "${n} pending security updates (dnf updateinfo list --security)"
        else
          ok "no pending security updates"
        fi
      else
        skip "could not query security updates"
      fi
    elif have apt-get; then
      n=$(apt-get -s upgrade 2>/dev/null | grep -c '^Inst.*security' || true)
      if (( ${n:-0} > 0 )); then
        warn "${n} pending security updates"
      else
        ok "no pending security updates"
      fi
    fi
  fi
  return 0
}

# Sets FW_KIND (firewalld|ufw|none) and FW_STATE (active|inactive|none) without printing.
detect_firewall() {
  FW_KIND=none; FW_STATE=none
  (( LIMITED == 0 )) || return 0
  if have firewall-cmd || unit_exists firewalld.service; then
    FW_KIND=firewalld; FW_STATE=inactive
    if have firewall-cmd && [[ "$(firewall-cmd --state 2>/dev/null || true)" == "running" ]]; then FW_STATE=active; fi
  elif have ufw; then
    FW_KIND=ufw; FW_STATE=inactive
    if ufw status 2>/dev/null | grep -qi '^Status: active'; then FW_STATE=active; fi
  fi
  return 0
}

check_firewall() {
  section "Firewall and brute-force protection"
  if (( LIMITED )); then
    skip "firewall and fail2ban: need root (run as root for the full report)"
    return 0
  fi
  local ports p ssh_ok=0 zone svc open ufw_out
  ports=$(sshd -T 2>/dev/null | awk '/^port / { print $2 }' | sort -un | tr '\n' ' ' || true)
  ports="${ports:-22}"

  case "$FW_KIND:$FW_STATE" in
    firewalld:active)
      zone=$(firewall-cmd --get-default-zone 2>/dev/null || true)
      svc=$(firewall-cmd --zone="$zone" --list-services 2>/dev/null || true)
      open=$(firewall-cmd --zone="$zone" --list-ports 2>/dev/null || true)
      for p in $ports; do
        if [[ " ${open} " == *" ${p}/tcp "* ]]; then ssh_ok=1; fi
        if [[ "$p" == "22" && " ${svc} " == *" ssh "* ]]; then ssh_ok=1; fi
      done
      if (( ssh_ok )); then
        ok "firewalld active, default zone ${zone:-?}, SSH allowed"
      else
        fail "firewalld active but SSH (${ports% }) is not allowed in zone ${zone:-?}; the next reload can lock you out"
      fi
      info "open in ${zone:-?}: services ${svc:-none} | ports ${open:-none}"
      ;;
    firewalld:*)
      if is_runner; then info "firewalld installed but not running (cloud firewall assumed)"
      else warn "firewalld installed but not running (systemctl enable --now firewalld)"; fi
      ;;
    ufw:active)
      ufw_out=$(ufw status 2>/dev/null || true)
      for p in $ports; do
        if grep -Eq "^(${p}(/tcp)?|OpenSSH|SSH)( \\(v6\\))?( on [^[:space:]]+)?[[:space:]]+(ALLOW|LIMIT)" <<<"$ufw_out"; then ssh_ok=1; fi
      done
      if (( ssh_ok )); then
        ok "ufw active, SSH allowed"
      else
        fail "ufw active but SSH (${ports% }) is not allowed"
      fi
      open=$(awk '/ALLOW/ && !/\(v6\)/ { print $1 }' <<<"$ufw_out" | sort -u | tr '\n' ' ')
````
