````bash
# Create the stack's containers and volumes without starting them, then hand the volumes to the service user.
chown_volumes() {
  local dir="$1" project v
  mon_ids
  project=$(cd "$dir" && docker compose config --format json | jq -r .name)
  (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start --quiet-pull >/dev/null 2>&1) || (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start >/dev/null)
  for v in $(docker volume ls -q --filter "label=com.docker.compose.project=${project}"); do
    docker run --rm --network none -v "${v}:/v" alpine:3.20 chown -R "${MON_UID}:${MON_GID}" /v
  done
}
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/lib.sh"

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/security-tools.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Security tools to audit, test and scan YOUR OWN servers. Use them only on systems you own or are authorized to test.
# No option = --audit --network --scan. Groups:
#   --audit    lynis, OpenSCAP (EL/Fedora, evaluation only, never --remediate), auditd with a minimal ruleset, ssh-audit, testssl.sh, debsums (Debian)
#   --network  nmap, tcpdump, tshark, iperf3, socat, ncat, whois, dig, lsof, mtr, traceroute, iftop, nethogs, bmon
#   --scan     trivy, gitleaks, hadolint, cosign, osv-scanner, trufflehog, dockle: official release binaries at pinned versions, checked against the checksums file of the same release
# Opt-in (not part of the default, they use RAM, disk IO or are noisy): --aide --clamav --rkhunter --chkrootkit --arp-scan --grype --syft --k8s
#   --kali     the `kali` helper (Kali in a Docker container; nothing offensive is installed on the host)
#   --audit-timer  weekly systemd timer: lynis, oscap, aide --check, rkhunter (whatever is installed) into /var/log/security-audit
# Every package is installed on its own: one that is missing on this release is logged and skipped. Safe to rerun.
# Supported: CentOS Stream / Rocky / AlmaLinux 10, Fedora 44, Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.04.
set -Eeuo pipefail
# shellcheck source=lib.sh
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"

# --- pinned versions of the release binaries (override with the same-named environment variable) -----------------
# Each is downloaded from the project's GitHub release page and compared with the checksums file published in the same
# release. That catches corrupt or truncated downloads; it does not protect against a replaced release, because the
# checksums come from the same place as the binary.
: "${TRIVY_VERSION:=0.76.1}"
: "${GRYPE_VERSION:=0.121.0}"
: "${SYFT_VERSION:=1.55.0}"
: "${GITLEAKS_VERSION:=8.30.1}"
: "${HADOLINT_VERSION:=2.14.0}"
: "${DOCKLE_VERSION:=0.4.15}"
: "${COSIGN_VERSION:=3.1.4}"
: "${OSV_SCANNER_VERSION:=2.7.0}"
: "${TRUFFLEHOG_VERSION:=3.99.7}"
: "${KUBESCAPE_VERSION:=4.0.17}"
: "${KUBE_BENCH_VERSION:=0.16.0}"
# ssh-audit from PyPI into /opt/ssh-audit (only when the distro has no package); the wheel hash is checked by pip.
: "${SSH_AUDIT_VERSION:=3.9.0}"
: "${SSH_AUDIT_WHEEL_SHA256:=3dad48d4678e0de31f6332cc7674f9d7dd1805f86babf3796f41c2fd879cb812}"
# testssl.sh: distro package where there is one; otherwise a git checkout of the tag, accepted only when the commit
# is the pinned one (a commit id is a content hash of the whole tree).
: "${TESTSSL_VERSION:=3.2.4}"
: "${TESTSSL_COMMIT:=97763a411c525720a5f9bd9d2cded416b10f210a}"

SEC_STATE=/var/lib/security-tools
AUDIT_LOG_DIR=/var/log/security-audit

WANT_AUDIT=0 WANT_NETWORK=0 WANT_SCAN=0 WANT_KALI=0 WANT_TIMER=0
WANT_AIDE=0 WANT_CLAMAV=0 WANT_RKHUNTER=0 WANT_CHKROOTKIT=0 WANT_ARP=0 WANT_GRYPE=0 WANT_SYFT=0 WANT_K8S=0

usage() {
  cat <<USAGE
Usage: $0 [--audit] [--network] [--scan] [opt-in tools] [--kali] [--audit-timer]

Installs security tools for testing and auditing systems you own or are authorized to test.
No option = --audit --network --scan. Any option given = only what you name.

  --audit        lynis, OpenSCAP (EL/Fedora; audit only, never remediates: CIS remediation breaks Docker forwarding),
                 auditd with a small ruleset (sudoers, sshd_config, runner config, docker; no execve; ~200 MB log cap),
                 ssh-audit, testssl.sh, debsums (Debian family)
  --network      nmap, tcpdump, tshark (nobody is added to the wireshark group), iperf3, socat, ncat, whois, dig,
                 lsof, mtr, traceroute, iftop, nethogs, bmon
  --scan         trivy ${TRIVY_VERSION}, gitleaks ${GITLEAKS_VERSION}, hadolint ${HADOLINT_VERSION}, cosign ${COSIGN_VERSION}, osv-scanner ${OSV_SCANNER_VERSION},
                 trufflehog ${TRUFFLEHOG_VERSION}, dockle ${DOCKLE_VERSION} (release binaries, SHA256 verified; a mismatch is refused)

Opt-in, because they cost RAM, IO or produce noise:
  --aide         AIDE file integrity, scoped to /etc /usr/local /boot /usr/bin /usr/sbin; the baseline scan runs at idle IO priority
  --clamav       clamscan on demand + a daily freshclam timer. Never the clamd daemon: a loaded signature set needs
                 ~1.2 GB RAM (clamscan needs about as much while it runs), a lot on a 4 GB runner host
  --rkhunter     rkhunter; baseline refreshed after package updates (apt hook; dnf action on EL). Noisy on Docker hosts
  --chkrootkit   chkrootkit. Overlaps with rkhunter, false positives on Docker hosts are common
  --arp-scan     arp-scan (little use on Hetzner's routed network)
  --grype --syft grype ${GRYPE_VERSION}, syft ${SYFT_VERSION} (trivy already does vulnerability scans and SBOMs)
  --k8s          kubescape ${KUBESCAPE_VERSION}, kube-bench ${KUBE_BENCH_VERSION} (only useful where you run Kubernetes)

  --kali         the 'kali' helper: Kali Linux in a Docker container, built on first use (2-3 GB); needs Docker
  --audit-timer  weekly timer (idle priority): lynis, oscap, aide --check, rkhunter, reports in ${AUDIT_LOG_DIR}
  --all          --audit --network --scan --kali and every opt-in tool except --audit-timer

Supported: CentOS Stream / Rocky / AlmaLinux 10, Fedora 44, Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.04.
USAGE
}

ANY=0
while (( $# )); do
  ANY=1
  case "$1" in
    --audit) WANT_AUDIT=1 ;;
    --network) WANT_NETWORK=1 ;;
    --scan) WANT_SCAN=1 ;;
    --kali) WANT_KALI=1 ;;
    --audit-timer) WANT_TIMER=1 ;;
    --aide) WANT_AIDE=1 ;;
    --clamav) WANT_CLAMAV=1 ;;
    --rkhunter) WANT_RKHUNTER=1 ;;
    --chkrootkit) WANT_CHKROOTKIT=1 ;;
    --arp-scan) WANT_ARP=1 ;;
    --grype) WANT_GRYPE=1 ;;
    --syft) WANT_SYFT=1 ;;
    --k8s) WANT_K8S=1 ;;
    --all) WANT_AUDIT=1 WANT_NETWORK=1 WANT_SCAN=1 WANT_KALI=1 WANT_AIDE=1 WANT_CLAMAV=1 WANT_RKHUNTER=1 WANT_CHKROOTKIT=1 WANT_ARP=1 WANT_GRYPE=1 WANT_SYFT=1 WANT_K8S=1 ;;
    -h|--help) usage; exit 0 ;;
    *) usage >&2; die "unknown option: $1" ;;
  esac
  shift
done
if (( ANY == 0 )); then
  WANT_AUDIT=1 WANT_NETWORK=1 WANT_SCAN=1
fi
require_root

# --- OS ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[[ -r /etc/os-release ]] || die "/etc/os-release missing, cannot detect the OS"
# shellcheck disable=SC1091
. /etc/os-release
case "${ID:-}:${VERSION_ID%%.*}" in
  centos:10|rocky:10|almalinux:10|rhel:10) FAMILY=el ;;
  fedora:44)                                FAMILY=fedora ;;
  debian:13|ubuntu:26)                      FAMILY=debian ;;
  *) die "unsupported OS ${PRETTY_NAME:-${ID:-unknown} ${VERSION_ID:-}}: this script supports CentOS Stream/Rocky/AlmaLinux 10, Fedora 44, Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.04" ;;
esac
case "$(uname -m)" in
  x86_64|amd64)  ARCH=amd64 ;;
  aarch64|arm64) ARCH=arm64 ;;
  *)             ARCH="" ;;
esac

INSTALLED=() PRESENT=() MISSING=() SKIPPED=()
CHECKSUM_FAIL=0
DL_TRIED=0   # downloads attempted in this run: release binaries, the testssl git clone, the ssh-audit pip install (tools already present are not counted)
DL_FAILED=0  # of those, how many were not installed (a scan run with no usable curl or architecture counts as one failure)

have_systemd() { [[ -d /run/systemd/system ]]; }

if [[ $FAMILY == debian ]]; then
  export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
  apt-get update -q >/dev/null || log "WARN: apt-get update failed, package lists may be stale"
  pm_installed() { [[ "$(dpkg-query -W -f='${Status}' "$1" 2>/dev/null || true)" == "install ok installed" ]]; }
  pm_install() { apt-get install -y -q --no-install-recommends "$1" >/dev/null 2>&1; }
else
  pm_installed() { rpm -q --quiet "$1"; }
  pm_install() { dnf -y -q install "$1" >/dev/null 2>&1; }
fi

# pkg_ensure NAME: 0 when installed (already or now), 1 when not available. Records the result.
pkg_ensure() {
  if pm_installed "$1"; then PRESENT+=("$1"); return 0; fi
  if pm_install "$1"; then INSTALLED+=("$1"); return 0; fi
  return 1
}

# want SPEC...: SPEC is a package or alternatives "a|b" (the first one that installs wins). Unavailable ones are logged at the end.
want() {
  local spec alt ok
  for spec in "$@"; do
    ok=0
    for alt in ${spec//|/ }; do
      if pkg_ensure "$alt"; then ok=1; break; fi
    done
    if (( ok == 0 )); then MISSING+=("${spec//|/ or }"); fi
  done
}

if [[ $FAMILY == el ]] && (( WANT_AUDIT || WANT_NETWORK || WANT_AIDE || WANT_CLAMAV || WANT_RKHUNTER || WANT_CHKROOTKIT || WANT_ARP )); then
  dnf -y -q install epel-release >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: epel-release not available, EPEL tools will be missing"
  # some EPEL packages need CRB; enabling it is the documented companion of EPEL
  if command -v crb >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    crb enable >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: could not enable CRB"
  else
    dnf -y -q config-manager --set-enabled crb >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: could not enable CRB (dnf config-manager)"
  fi
fi

# --- audit -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
AIDE_DIR=/var/lib/security-tools/aide
AIDE_CONF=/etc/security-tools/aide.conf

# Own AIDE config, scoped to the places that matter. The distro default (whole filesystem, daily run) is not used.
setup_aide() {
  command -v aide >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  install -d -m 0750 /etc/security-tools "$AIDE_DIR"
  cat > "$AIDE_CONF" <<EOF
# Managed by common/security-tools.sh (scoped baseline: no containers, no CI caches, no home directories)
database_in=file:${AIDE_DIR}/aide.db.gz
database_out=file:${AIDE_DIR}/aide.db.new.gz
gzip_dbout=yes
report_url=stdout
NORMAL = p+i+n+u+g+s+m+c+sha256
/etc NORMAL
/etc/gitlab-runner NORMAL
/usr/local NORMAL
/boot NORMAL
/usr/bin NORMAL
/usr/sbin NORMAL
!/etc/mtab
!/etc/resolv.conf
!/etc/adjtime
!/etc/security-tools
!/usr/local/share/man
!/var/lib/docker
!/home/gitlab-runner
!/srv/ci-cache
!/opt/[^/]+/data
EOF
  chmod 600 "$AIDE_CONF"
  # Debian's own daily AIDE check would run the default whole-filesystem config
  if have_systemd; then
    systemctl disable --now dailyaidecheck.timer >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
  if [[ -s "${AIDE_DIR}/aide.db.gz" ]]; then
    log "aide: baseline already present (${AIDE_DIR}/aide.db.gz)"
    return 0
  fi
  log "aide: creating the baseline (idle IO priority, a few minutes)"
  nice -n 19 ionice -c3 aide --config "$AIDE_CONF" --init >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  if [[ -s "${AIDE_DIR}/aide.db.new.gz" ]]; then
    mv -f "${AIDE_DIR}/aide.db.new.gz" "${AIDE_DIR}/aide.db.gz"
    chmod 600 "${AIDE_DIR}/aide.db.gz"
    log "aide: baseline ready; check with: aide --config ${AIDE_CONF} --check. After planned changes run --update and replace the database"
  else
    log "WARN: aide --init produced no database; run it by hand: aide --config ${AIDE_CONF} --init"
  fi
}

# Minimal ruleset: configuration that decides who can get in. No execve logging (volume), no -e 2 (stays reloadable).
setup_auditd() {
  command -v auditctl >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  local f=/etc/audit/rules.d/60-security-tools.rules rules conf=/etc/audit/auditd.conf
  rules='# Managed by common/security-tools.sh. Not immutable (no -e 2). Paths that did not exist at install time are left out.
-w /etc/sudoers -p wa -k sudoers
-w /etc/sudoers.d/ -p wa -k sudoers
-w /etc/ssh/sshd_config -p wa -k sshd
-w /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/ -p wa -k sshd'
  # rerun this script after adding Docker or the runner to pick their paths up
  if [[ -d /etc/gitlab-runner ]]; then rules+=$'\n''-w /etc/gitlab-runner/ -p wa -k gitlab-runner'; fi
  if [[ -S /run/docker.sock ]]; then rules+=$'\n''-w /run/docker.sock -p rwa -k docker-sock'; fi
  install -d /etc/audit/rules.d
  if [[ "$(cat "$f" 2>/dev/null || true)" != "$rules" ]]; then
    printf '%s\n' "$rules" > "$f"
    chmod 640 "$f"
    log "auditd: wrote ${f}"
  fi
  # about 200 MB at most: 4 logs of 50 MB, rotated
  if [[ -f "$conf" ]]; then
    sed -i -E 's/^max_log_file[[:space:]]*=.*/max_log_file = 50/; s/^num_logs[[:space:]]*=.*/num_logs = 4/; s/^max_log_file_action[[:space:]]*=.*/max_log_file_action = ROTATE/' "$conf"
  fi
  if have_systemd; then
    systemctl enable --now auditd >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: could not enable auditd"
    augenrules --load >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: could not load the audit rules now (they apply at the next start of auditd)"
  else
    log "auditd: no systemd here, rules are written and load when auditd starts"
  fi
}

# clamscan on demand; signatures refreshed by a daily timer. The clamd daemon and the packaged freshclam daemon stay off.
setup_clamav() {
  command -v clamscan >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  # EL/Fedora ship freshclam.conf with 'Example', which makes freshclam refuse to run
  if [[ -f /etc/freshclam.conf ]] && grep -q '^Example' /etc/freshclam.conf; then
    sed -i -E 's/^Example/#Example/' /etc/freshclam.conf
  fi
  command -v freshclam >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  if have_systemd; then
    systemctl disable --now clamav-freshclam.service clamav-daemon.service clamd@scan.service >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
  if ! compgen -G '/var/lib/clamav/*.c[lv]d' >/dev/null; then
    log "clamav: fetching signatures (freshclam)"
    timeout 300 freshclam --quiet >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: freshclam failed, run it by hand before 'clamscan'"
  fi
  local u
  for u in security-freshclam.service security-freshclam.timer; do
    install -m 0644 "$REPO_ROOT/common/systemd/$u" "/etc/systemd/system/$u"
    restorecon_if "/etc/systemd/system/$u"
  done
  if have_systemd; then
    systemctl daemon-reload
    systemctl enable --now security-freshclam.timer >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: could not enable security-freshclam.timer"
  fi
}

# rkhunter compares file hashes with a baseline; without a refresh after package updates every update is a warning.
setup_rkhunter() {
  command -v rkhunter >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  if [[ ! -s /var/lib/rkhunter/db/rkhunter.dat ]]; then
    rkhunter --propupd >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: rkhunter --propupd failed"
  fi
  if [[ $FAMILY == debian ]]; then
    if [[ -f /etc/default/rkhunter ]]; then
      sed -i -E 's/^#?APT_AUTOGEN=.*/APT_AUTOGEN="true"/; s/^#?CRON_DAILY_RUN=.*/CRON_DAILY_RUN="false"/' /etc/default/rkhunter
    fi
  elif [[ $FAMILY == el ]]; then
    pkg_ensure python3-dnf-plugin-post-transaction-actions || true
    if [[ -d /etc/dnf/plugins/post-transaction-actions.d ]]; then
      echo '*:any:/usr/bin/rkhunter --propupd' > /etc/dnf/plugins/post-transaction-actions.d/rkhunter.action
    else
      log "WARN: dnf post-transaction-actions plugin missing: run 'rkhunter --propupd' after package updates"
    fi
  else
    log "rkhunter: run 'rkhunter --propupd' after package updates (no dnf hook is installed on Fedora)"
  fi
}

setup_ssh_audit() {
  if command -v ssh-audit >/dev/null 2>&1 && [[ ! -x /opt/ssh-audit/venv/bin/ssh-audit ]]; then
    PRESENT+=(ssh-audit); return 0
  fi
  if [[ ! -x /opt/ssh-audit/venv/bin/ssh-audit ]] && pkg_ensure ssh-audit; then return 0; fi
  if [[ -f "${SEC_STATE}/ssh-audit.version" && "$(cat "${SEC_STATE}/ssh-audit.version")" == "$SSH_AUDIT_VERSION" ]]; then
    PRESENT+=("ssh-audit ${SSH_AUDIT_VERSION}"); return 0
  fi
  command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_ensure python3 || { MISSING+=("ssh-audit (no package, no python3)"); return 0; }
  [[ $FAMILY == debian ]] && { pkg_ensure python3-venv || true; }
  local req
  DL_TRIED=$((DL_TRIED + 1))
  req=$(mktemp)
  printf 'ssh-audit==%s --hash=sha256:%s\n' "$SSH_AUDIT_VERSION" "$SSH_AUDIT_WHEEL_SHA256" > "$req"
  if rm -rf /opt/ssh-audit/venv && install -d /opt/ssh-audit && python3 -m venv /opt/ssh-audit/venv >/dev/null 2>&1 \
     && /opt/ssh-audit/venv/bin/pip install -q --no-cache-dir --require-hashes --only-binary :all: --no-deps -r "$req" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    printf '#!/bin/sh\nexec /opt/ssh-audit/venv/bin/ssh-audit "$@"\n' > /usr/local/bin/ssh-audit
    chmod 755 /usr/local/bin/ssh-audit
    restorecon_if /usr/local/bin/ssh-audit
    install -d "$SEC_STATE"
    echo "$SSH_AUDIT_VERSION" > "${SEC_STATE}/ssh-audit.version"
    INSTALLED+=("ssh-audit ${SSH_AUDIT_VERSION} (venv)")
  else
    DL_FAILED=$((DL_FAILED + 1))
    MISSING+=("ssh-audit (pip install failed)")
  fi
  rm -f "$req"
}

setup_testssl() {
  if command -v testssl >/dev/null 2>&1 \
     || { [[ -x /opt/testssl/testssl.sh ]] && [[ "$(cat "${SEC_STATE}/testssl.version" 2>/dev/null || true)" == "$TESTSSL_VERSION" ]]; }; then
    PRESENT+=(testssl); return 0
  fi
  if pkg_ensure testssl.sh || pkg_ensure testssl; then
    if ! command -v testssl >/dev/null 2>&1 && command -v testssl.sh >/dev/null 2>&1; then
      ln -sfn "$(command -v testssl.sh)" /usr/local/bin/testssl
    fi
    return 0
  fi
  command -v git >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_ensure git || { SKIPPED+=("testssl.sh (no package, no git)"); return 0; }
  local tmp
  DL_TRIED=$((DL_TRIED + 1))
  tmp=$(mktemp -d)
  if ! git clone -q --depth 1 --branch "v${TESTSSL_VERSION}" https://github.com/testssl/testssl.sh.git "$tmp/t" 2>/dev/null; then
    DL_FAILED=$((DL_FAILED + 1))
    SKIPPED+=("testssl.sh ${TESTSSL_VERSION} (git clone failed)")
  elif [[ "$(git -C "$tmp/t" rev-parse HEAD)" != "$TESTSSL_COMMIT" ]]; then
    log "ERROR: testssl.sh v${TESTSSL_VERSION} is not commit ${TESTSSL_COMMIT}, refusing to install"
    CHECKSUM_FAIL=1
  else
    rm -rf /opt/testssl
    install -d /opt/testssl
    rm -rf "$tmp/t/.git"
    cp -a "$tmp/t/." /opt/testssl/
    printf '#!/bin/sh\nexec /opt/testssl/testssl.sh "$@"\n' > /usr/local/bin/testssl
    chmod 755 /usr/local/bin/testssl
    restorecon_if /usr/local/bin/testssl
    install -d "$SEC_STATE"
    echo "$TESTSSL_VERSION" > "${SEC_STATE}/testssl.version"
    INSTALLED+=("testssl ${TESTSSL_VERSION}")
  fi
  rm -rf "$tmp"
}

fetch() { curl -fsSL --retry 3 --retry-delay 2 --connect-timeout 15 --max-time 900 -o "$2" "$1"; }

install_audit() {
  log "=== audit tools ==="
  local pk=(lynis)
  if [[ $FAMILY == debian ]]; then
    pk+=(auditd debsums)
  else
    pk+=(audit openscap-scanner scap-security-guide)
  fi
  want "${pk[@]}"
  setup_auditd
  setup_ssh_audit
  setup_testssl
  local ds
  ds=$(compgen -G '/usr/share/xml/scap/ssg/content/ssg-*-ds.xml' | head -1 || true)
  if [[ -n "$ds" ]]; then log "openscap: datastream ${ds} (profiles: oscap info ${ds}); evaluation only, never --remediate"; fi
}

install_optional() {
  log "=== opt-in tools ==="
  if (( WANT_AIDE )); then want aide; setup_aide; fi
  if (( WANT_CLAMAV )); then
    if [[ $FAMILY == debian ]]; then want clamav clamav-freshclam; else want clamav "clamav-freshclam|clamav-update"; fi
    setup_clamav
  fi
  if (( WANT_RKHUNTER )); then want rkhunter; setup_rkhunter; fi
  if (( WANT_CHKROOTKIT )); then want chkrootkit; fi
  if (( WANT_ARP )); then want arp-scan; fi
}

# --- network -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
install_network() {
  log "=== network tools ==="
  if [[ $FAMILY == debian ]]; then
    # tshark asks whether non-root users may capture; answer no without a prompt
    if command -v debconf-set-selections >/dev/null 2>&1; then
      echo 'wireshark-common wireshark-common/install-setuid boolean false' | debconf-set-selections || true
    fi
    want nmap tcpdump tshark iperf3 socat "ncat|netcat-openbsd" whois dnsutils lsof "mtr|mtr-tiny" traceroute iftop nethogs "bmon|nload"
  else
    want nmap tcpdump wireshark-cli iperf3 socat nmap-ncat whois bind-utils lsof mtr traceroute iftop nethogs "bmon|nload"
  fi
}

# --- release binaries --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# release_tool NAME VERSION REPO ASSET SUMS_FILES MODE [BIN]
#   ASSET: file name of the release asset for this arch (empty: no build for this arch)
#   SUMS_FILES: candidate names of the checksums file in the same release
#   MODE: tar (binary inside a tarball), bin (the asset is the binary), tarcfg (tarball with config dir, kept in /opt/NAME)
# Returns 0 on success or skip, 2 on checksum mismatch; the asset is never installed unless its SHA256 matches.
release_tool() {
  local name="$1" ver="$2" repo="$3" asset="$4" sums_list="$5" mode="$6" bin="${7:-$1}"
  local marker="${SEC_STATE}/${name}.version" tmp rc=0
  if [[ -z "$asset" ]]; then
    SKIPPED+=("${name} (no ${ARCH:-$(uname -m)} build)")
    return 0
  fi
  if [[ "$(cat "$marker" 2>/dev/null || true)" == "$ver" && -x "/usr/local/bin/${bin}" ]]; then
    PRESENT+=("${name} ${ver}")
    return 0
  fi
  DL_TRIED=$((DL_TRIED + 1))
  tmp=$(mktemp -d)
  release_fetch_install "$tmp" "$name" "$ver" "$repo" "$asset" "$sums_list" "$mode" "$bin" || rc=$?
  rm -rf "$tmp"
  case "$rc" in
    0) INSTALLED+=("${name} ${ver}"); install -d "$SEC_STATE"; echo "$ver" > "$marker" ;;
    2) CHECKSUM_FAIL=1; DL_FAILED=$((DL_FAILED + 1)); SKIPPED+=("${name} ${ver} (CHECKSUM MISMATCH, not installed)") ;;
    *) DL_FAILED=$((DL_FAILED + 1)); SKIPPED+=("${name} ${ver} (not downloaded)") ;;
  esac
  return 0
}

release_fetch_install() {
  local tmp="$1" name="$2" ver="$3" repo="$4" asset="$5" sums_list="$6" mode="$7" bin="$8"
  local base="https://github.com/${repo}/releases/download/v${ver}" s got="" want_sum have_sum src
  if ! fetch "${base}/${asset}" "${tmp}/${asset}"; then
    log "WARN: ${name} ${ver}: cannot download ${base}/${asset}"
    return 1
  fi
  for s in $sums_list; do
    if fetch "${base}/${s}" "${tmp}/sums" 2>/dev/null; then got=1; break; fi
  done
  if [[ -z "$got" ]]; then
    log "WARN: ${name} ${ver}: no checksums file (${sums_list}) in the release, refusing to install unverified"
    return 1
  fi
  want_sum=$(awk -v f="$asset" '{ n = $2; sub(/^\*/, "", n); if (n == f) print $1 }' "${tmp}/sums")
  if [[ -z "$want_sum" && "$(grep -c . "${tmp}/sums")" -eq 1 ]]; then
    want_sum=$(awk '{ print $1 }' "${tmp}/sums")   # single-asset .sha256 file
  fi
  if [[ ! "$want_sum" =~ ^[0-9a-fA-F]{64}$ ]]; then
    log "WARN: ${name} ${ver}: ${asset} has no usable entry in the checksums file, refusing to install"
    return 1
  fi
  have_sum=$(sha256sum "${tmp}/${asset}" | awk '{ print $1 }')
  if [[ "${have_sum,,}" != "${want_sum,,}" ]]; then
    log "ERROR: ${name} ${ver}: SHA256 MISMATCH for ${asset} (published ${want_sum}, got ${have_sum}); refusing to install"
    return 2
  fi
  case "$mode" in
    bin)
      install -m 0755 "${tmp}/${asset}" "/usr/local/bin/${bin}"
      ;;
    tar)
      install -d "${tmp}/x"
      tar -xzf "${tmp}/${asset}" -C "${tmp}/x"
      src=$(find "${tmp}/x" -type f -name "$bin" | head -1)
      [[ -n "$src" ]] || { log "WARN: ${name}: ${bin} not found in ${asset}"; return 1; }
      install -m 0755 "$src" "/usr/local/bin/${bin}"
      ;;
    tarcfg)
      install -d "${tmp}/x"
      tar -xzf "${tmp}/${asset}" -C "${tmp}/x"
      [[ -f "${tmp}/x/${bin}" ]] || { log "WARN: ${name}: ${bin} not found in ${asset}"; return 1; }
      rm -rf "/opt/${name}"
      install -d "/opt/${name}"
      cp -a "${tmp}/x/." "/opt/${name}/"
      chmod 755 "/opt/${name}/${bin}"
      printf '#!/bin/sh\nexec /opt/%s/%s --config-dir /opt/%s/cfg --config /opt/%s/cfg/config.yaml "$@"\n' \
        "$name" "$bin" "$name" "$name" > "/usr/local/bin/${bin}"
      chmod 755 "/usr/local/bin/${bin}"
      ;;
  esac
  restorecon_if "/usr/local/bin/${bin}"
  return 0
}

install_scan() {
  log "=== scanners (release binaries, SHA256 verified) ==="
  if [[ -z "$ARCH" ]]; then
    DL_TRIED=$((DL_TRIED + 1)); DL_FAILED=$((DL_FAILED + 1))
    SKIPPED+=("scan tools (unsupported architecture $(uname -m))")
    return 0
  fi
  command -v curl >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_ensure curl || { DL_TRIED=$((DL_TRIED + 1)); DL_FAILED=$((DL_FAILED + 1)); SKIPPED+=("scan tools (curl missing)"); return 0; }
  local a="$ARCH" big small x
  if [[ $ARCH == amd64 ]]; then big=64bit; small=x86_64; x=x64; else big=ARM64; small=arm64; x=arm64; fi
  if (( WANT_SCAN )); then
    release_tool trivy "$TRIVY_VERSION" aquasecurity/trivy "trivy_${TRIVY_VERSION}_Linux-${big}.tar.gz" \
      "trivy_${TRIVY_VERSION}_checksums.txt" tar
    release_tool gitleaks "$GITLEAKS_VERSION" gitleaks/gitleaks "gitleaks_${GITLEAKS_VERSION}_linux_${x}.tar.gz" \
      "gitleaks_${GITLEAKS_VERSION}_checksums.txt" tar
    release_tool hadolint "$HADOLINT_VERSION" hadolint/hadolint "hadolint-linux-${small}" \
      "hadolint-linux-${small}.sha256" bin
    release_tool cosign "$COSIGN_VERSION" sigstore/cosign "cosign-linux-${a}" \
      "cosign_checksums.txt" bin cosign
    release_tool osv-scanner "$OSV_SCANNER_VERSION" google/osv-scanner "osv-scanner_linux_${a}" \
      "osv-scanner_SHA256SUMS" bin
    release_tool trufflehog "$TRUFFLEHOG_VERSION" trufflesecurity/trufflehog "trufflehog_${TRUFFLEHOG_VERSION}_linux_${a}.tar.gz" \
      "trufflehog_${TRUFFLEHOG_VERSION}_checksums.txt" tar
    release_tool dockle "$DOCKLE_VERSION" goodwithtech/dockle "dockle_${DOCKLE_VERSION}_Linux-${big}.tar.gz" \
      "dockle_${DOCKLE_VERSION}_checksums.txt" tar
  fi
  if (( WANT_GRYPE )); then
    release_tool grype "$GRYPE_VERSION" anchore/grype "grype_${GRYPE_VERSION}_linux_${a}.tar.gz" \
      "grype_${GRYPE_VERSION}_checksums.txt" tar
  fi
  if (( WANT_SYFT )); then
    release_tool syft "$SYFT_VERSION" anchore/syft "syft_${SYFT_VERSION}_linux_${a}.tar.gz" \
      "syft_${SYFT_VERSION}_checksums.txt" tar
  fi
  if (( WANT_K8S )); then
    release_tool kubescape "$KUBESCAPE_VERSION" kubescape/kubescape "kubescape_${KUBESCAPE_VERSION}_linux_${a}.tar.gz" \
      "checksums.sha256 kubescape_${KUBESCAPE_VERSION}_checksums.txt checksums.txt" tar
    release_tool kube-bench "$KUBE_BENCH_VERSION" aquasecurity/kube-bench "kube-bench_${KUBE_BENCH_VERSION}_linux_${a}.tar.gz" \
      "kube-bench_${KUBE_BENCH_VERSION}_checksums.txt checksums.txt" tarcfg
  fi
}

# --- kali helper -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
install_kali() {
  log "=== kali helper (container only) ==="
  install -m 0755 "$REPO_ROOT/common/kali" /usr/local/bin/kali
  install -d -m 0755 /usr/local/share/kali-tools
  install -m 0644 "$REPO_ROOT/common/security/kali/Dockerfile" /usr/local/share/kali-tools/Dockerfile
  restorecon_if /usr/local/bin/kali /usr/local/share/kali-tools/Dockerfile
  INSTALLED+=("kali helper")
  command -v docker >/dev/null 2>&1 || log "WARN: docker is not installed; 'kali' needs it (this script does not install Docker)"
}

# --- audit timer -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
install_timer() {
  log "=== weekly audit timer ==="
  install -d -m 0750 "$AUDIT_LOG_DIR"
  install -m 0755 "$REPO_ROOT/common/security-audit-run.sh" /usr/local/sbin/security-audit-run
  local u
  for u in security-audit.service security-audit.timer; do
    install -m 0644 "$REPO_ROOT/common/systemd/$u" "/etc/systemd/system/$u"
    restorecon_if "/etc/systemd/system/$u"
  done
  restorecon_if /usr/local/sbin/security-audit-run
  if have_systemd; then
    systemctl daemon-reload
    systemctl enable --now security-audit.timer >/dev/null
    systemctl list-timers security-audit.timer --no-pager | head -3
  else
    log "WARN: no systemd here, timer files installed but not enabled"
  fi
}

(( WANT_AUDIT ))   && install_audit
(( WANT_AIDE || WANT_CLAMAV || WANT_RKHUNTER || WANT_CHKROOTKIT || WANT_ARP )) && install_optional
(( WANT_NETWORK )) && install_network
(( WANT_SCAN || WANT_GRYPE || WANT_SYFT || WANT_K8S )) && install_scan
(( WANT_KALI ))    && install_kali
(( WANT_TIMER ))   && install_timer

log "installed now (${#INSTALLED[@]}): ${INSTALLED[*]:-}"
log "already present (${#PRESENT[@]}): ${PRESENT[*]:-}"
if (( ${#MISSING[@]} )); then log "WARN: not available on this release (${#MISSING[@]}): ${MISSING[*]}"; fi
if (( ${#SKIPPED[@]} )); then log "WARN: skipped (${#SKIPPED[@]}): ${SKIPPED[*]}"; fi
for c in lynis rkhunter chkrootkit aide auditctl clamscan oscap ssh-audit testssl nmap tcpdump tshark iperf3 socat ncat \
         trivy grype syft gitleaks hadolint dockle cosign osv-scanner trufflehog kubescape kube-bench kali; do
  if command -v "$c" >/dev/null 2>&1; then printf '  %-12s ok\n' "$c"; fi
done
log "Use these tools only on systems you own or are authorized to test."
if (( CHECKSUM_FAIL )); then
  die "at least one download failed its SHA256 check and was not installed (see ERROR lines above)"
fi
if (( DL_TRIED > 0 && DL_FAILED == DL_TRIED )); then
  die "every download failed (${DL_FAILED} of ${DL_TRIED}), nothing was installed (see WARN lines above)"
fi
if (( DL_FAILED > 0 )); then
  log "WARN: ${DL_FAILED} of ${DL_TRIED} downloads failed, the others were installed"
fi
log "OK"
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/security-tools.sh"

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/kali" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# kali: Kali Linux tools in a Docker container, nothing offensive is installed on the host.
# Use only against systems you own or are authorized to test.
set -Eeuo pipefail

IMAGE="${KALI_IMAGE_NAME:-local/kali-tools}"
VOLUME="${KALI_VOLUME:-kali-home}"
HOME_IN=/home/kali

usage() {
  cat <<'USAGE'
Usage: kali [--host-net] [COMMAND [ARGS...]]
       kali update | remove [-y] | --help

Runs Kali Linux (kalilinux/kali-rolling, headless tool set) in a container that is deleted on exit.
Your files in /home/kali live in the Docker volume "kali-home" and survive between runs.

  kali                       interactive shell
  kali nmap -sV 203.0.113.5  run one command (use sudo inside for raw-socket scans: kali sudo nmap -sS ...)
  --host-net                 use the host network (default: Docker bridge). Gives the container the host's
                             network view; only use it when you need it
  update                     pull the newest base image and rebuild (the volume is kept)
  remove [-y]                delete the image and the volume (asks first unless -y)

The first run builds the image local/kali-tools: 2-3 GB download, several minutes. No Docker socket is mounted.
Needs Docker (not installed by this tool) and permission to use it (root, or the docker group).
USAGE
}

reminder() { echo "Reminder: use these tools only on systems you own or are authorized to test." >&2; }
die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

find_dockerfile_dir() {
  local here d
  here="$(cd "$(dirname "$(readlink -f "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")")" && pwd)"
  for d in "${KALI_DIR:-}" /usr/local/share/kali-tools "${here}/security/kali"; do
    if [[ -n "$d" && -f "$d/Dockerfile" ]]; then (cd "$d" && pwd); return 0; fi
  done
  die "Dockerfile not found (looked in /usr/local/share/kali-tools and common/security/kali); run common/security-tools.sh --kali or set KALI_DIR"
}

need_docker() {
  command -v docker >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "docker is not installed. This helper needs Docker and does not install it."
  docker info >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "cannot talk to the Docker daemon: is it running, and are you root or in the docker group? (try: sudo kali)"
}

have_image() { docker image inspect "$IMAGE" >/dev/null 2>&1; }

build() {
  local dir
  dir="$(find_dockerfile_dir)"
  echo "Building ${IMAGE} from the official kalilinux/kali-rolling image (2-3 GB, several minutes)..." >&2
  docker build "$@" -t "$IMAGE" "$dir"
}

main() {
  local host_net=0 yes=0 args=()
  case "${1:-}" in
    -h|--help) usage; reminder; exit 0 ;;
    update)
      reminder; need_docker
      build --pull --no-cache
      echo "Updated ${IMAGE}; the volume ${VOLUME} was kept." >&2
      exit 0 ;;
    remove)
      reminder; need_docker
      [[ "${2:-}" == "-y" ]] && yes=1
      if (( ! yes )); then
        [[ -t 0 ]] || die "not a terminal: use 'kali remove -y'"
        read -r -p "Delete image ${IMAGE} and volume ${VOLUME} (your files in ${HOME_IN})? [y/N] " ans
        [[ "$ans" == y || "$ans" == Y ]] || { echo "Nothing deleted."; exit 0; }
      fi
      docker image rm "$IMAGE" >/dev/null 2>&1 || echo "image ${IMAGE} not present" >&2
      docker volume rm "$VOLUME" >/dev/null 2>&1 || echo "volume ${VOLUME} not present" >&2
      echo "Removed."
      exit 0 ;;
  esac
  if [[ "${1:-}" == "--host-net" ]]; then host_net=1; shift; fi
  args=("$@")

  reminder
  need_docker
  have_image || build
  local run=(docker run --rm -i --hostname kali -v "${VOLUME}:${HOME_IN}")
  if [[ -t 0 && -t 1 ]]; then run+=(-t); fi
  if (( host_net )); then run+=(--network host); fi
  exec "${run[@]}" "$IMAGE" "${args[@]}"
}

main "$@"
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 "$R/common/kali"

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/security-audit-run.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Weekly audit run by security-audit.timer: lynis, OpenSCAP (evaluation only), aide --check and rkhunter, whichever is installed.
# Reports go to /var/log/security-audit/<tool>-<timestamp>.log; the newest KEEP runs of each tool are kept.
# Nothing is remediated. A tool that fails or finds problems never stops the others.
set -uo pipefail

DIR=/var/log/security-audit
KEEP="${KEEP:-8}"
AIDE_CONF=/etc/security-tools/aide.conf
OSCAP_PROFILE="${OSCAP_PROFILE:-xccdf_org.ssgproject.content_profile_standard}"
OSCAP_DATASTREAM="${OSCAP_DATASTREAM:-}"
TS=$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)

install -d -m 0750 "$DIR"

# prune NAME: delete all but the newest KEEP logs of NAME and the files sharing their timestamp
prune() {
  local old f
  while IFS= read -r old; do
    [[ -n "$old" ]] || continue
    for f in "${old%.log}".*; do rm -f -- "$f"; done
  done < <(ls -1t "$DIR/$1"-*.log 2>/dev/null | tail -n +"$((KEEP + 1))")
}

# run_one NAME COMMAND...: output to the log file, exit code to the journal
run_one() {
  local name="$1" rc=0
  shift
  "$@" > "$DIR/${name}-${TS}.log" 2>&1 || rc=$?
  echo "${name}: exit ${rc}, log ${DIR}/${name}-${TS}.log"
  prune "$name"
}

if command -v lynis >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  run_one lynis lynis audit system --cronjob
fi

if command -v oscap >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  if [[ -z "$OSCAP_DATASTREAM" ]]; then
    OSCAP_DATASTREAM=$(compgen -G '/usr/share/xml/scap/ssg/content/ssg-*-ds.xml' | head -1 || true)
  fi
  if [[ -n "$OSCAP_DATASTREAM" ]]; then
    run_one oscap oscap xccdf eval --profile "$OSCAP_PROFILE" --results "$DIR/oscap-${TS}.xml" --report "$DIR/oscap-${TS}.html" "$OSCAP_DATASTREAM"
  fi
fi

if command -v aide >/dev/null 2>&1 && [[ -s /var/lib/security-tools/aide/aide.db.gz && -f "$AIDE_CONF" ]]; then
  run_one aide aide --config "$AIDE_CONF" --check
fi

if command -v rkhunter >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  run_one rkhunter rkhunter --check --sk --rwo
fi
exit 0
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/security-audit-run.sh"

install -d "$R/common/security/kali"
cat > "$R/common/security/kali/Dockerfile" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
# Kali Linux toolbox for testing systems you own or are authorized to test. Built on demand by common/kali (`kali`).
# Base: the official Kali image. It only has the rolling tag `latest` (no dated tags): `kali update` rebuilds on the newest.
# Override with --build-arg KALI_IMAGE=kalilinux/kali-rolling@sha256:<digest> to pin a digest.
ARG KALI_IMAGE=kalilinux/kali-rolling:latest
FROM ${KALI_IMAGE}

ENV DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive

# kali-linux-headless: the command-line tools (nmap, nikto, sqlmap, hydra, ...). Several GB.
# hadolint ignore=DL3008
RUN apt-get update \
 && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends kali-linux-headless sudo ca-certificates \
 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Non-root default user; sudo inside the container for the tools that need raw sockets (nmap -sS). No password, no keys.
RUN useradd --create-home --shell /bin/bash --uid 1000 kali \
 && echo 'kali ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD:ALL' > /etc/sudoers.d/kali \
 && chmod 0440 /etc/sudoers.d/kali

USER kali
WORKDIR /home/kali
CMD ["/bin/bash"]
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 "$R/common/security/kali/Dockerfile"

install -d "$R/common/systemd"
cat > "$R/common/systemd/security-audit.service" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
[Unit]
Description=Weekly security audit (lynis, oscap, aide, rkhunter)
Documentation=file:///usr/local/sbin/security-audit-run

[Service]
Type=oneshot
SyslogIdentifier=security-audit
EnvironmentFile=-/etc/default/security-audit
ExecStart=/usr/local/sbin/security-audit-run
Nice=19
IOSchedulingClass=idle
CPUSchedulingPolicy=idle
TimeoutStartSec=4h
# Audit tools only read the system and write their reports.
NoNewPrivileges=yes
PrivateTmp=yes
ProtectSystem=strict
ProtectHome=read-only
ProtectKernelModules=yes
ProtectControlGroups=yes
ReadWritePaths=/var/log /var/lib/security-tools -/var/lib/rkhunter -/var/lib/lynis /run
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 "$R/common/systemd/security-audit.service"

install -d "$R/common/systemd"
cat > "$R/common/systemd/security-audit.timer" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
[Unit]
Description=Run the security audit every Sunday

[Timer]
OnCalendar=Sun *-*-* 04:20:00
Persistent=true
RandomizedDelaySec=30m

[Install]
WantedBy=timers.target
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 "$R/common/systemd/security-audit.timer"

install -d "$R/common/systemd"
cat > "$R/common/systemd/security-freshclam.service" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
[Unit]
Description=Update ClamAV signatures (freshclam, no daemon)

[Service]
Type=oneshot
SyslogIdentifier=security-freshclam
ExecStart=/usr/bin/freshclam --quiet
Nice=19
IOSchedulingClass=idle
NoNewPrivileges=yes
PrivateTmp=yes
ProtectSystem=strict
ProtectHome=yes
ReadWritePaths=/var/lib/clamav /var/log
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 "$R/common/systemd/security-freshclam.service"

install -d "$R/common/systemd"
cat > "$R/common/systemd/security-freshclam.timer" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
[Unit]
Description=Update ClamAV signatures daily

[Timer]
OnCalendar=*-*-* 05:10:00
Persistent=true
RandomizedDelaySec=2h

[Install]
WantedBy=timers.target
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 644 "$R/common/systemd/security-freshclam.timer"

echo "files written to $R: 9"
bash "$R/common/security-tools.sh" </dev/null
INSTALL
````

<a id="register"></a>
## Register the runner

Asks for the `glrt-` token (GitLab: Settings > CI/CD > Runners > New runner). Change `DESCRIPTION` if you like.

````bash
sudo bash <<'INSTALL'
set -Eeuo pipefail
R=/root/server-init-config

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/lib.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Shared helpers for the installers (runner/install, server and common). Sourced, not executed.
# shellcheck shell=bash

REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
export REPO_ROOT

log() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

require_root() { [[ $EUID -eq 0 ]] || die "run as root"; }

selinux_on() { command -v selinuxenabled >/dev/null 2>&1 && selinuxenabled; }

pkg_install() {
  if command -v dnf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    dnf -y -q install "$@"
  elif command -v apt-get >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    apt-get update -q && apt-get install -y -q "$@"
  else
    die "no supported package manager"
  fi
}

# Label a directory so containers may read files in it (no-op without SELinux).
selinux_label_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  install -d -m 0755 "$dir"
  selinux_on || return 0
  command -v semanage >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_install policycoreutils-python-utils
  # Read the whole list first: grep -q exits at the first match and would SIGPIPE the producer under pipefail.
  if ! grep -q "^${dir}(/\.\*)?" <<<"$(semanage fcontext -l)"; then
    semanage fcontext -a -t container_file_t "${dir}(/.*)?"
  fi
  restorecon -R "$dir"
}

restorecon_if() { if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon "$@" || true; fi; }

# RHEL-family sudo resets PATH to secure_path, which can leave out /usr/local/bin (so `sudo health` fails).
# Adds a validated drop-in unless some sudoers file already sets a secure_path that includes it.
ensure_secure_path() {
  local f=/etc/sudoers.d/50-secure-path tmp
  command -v visudo >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  if grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path[[:space:]]*=.*/usr/local/bin' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0
  fi
  if ! grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0   # no secure_path at all (Debian family keeps it in sudoers; others use the caller PATH)
  fi
  tmp=$(mktemp)
  printf 'Defaults secure_path="/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"\n' > "$tmp"
  if visudo -cqf "$tmp"; then
    install -d -m 0750 /etc/sudoers.d
    install -m 0440 -o root -g root "$tmp" "$f"
    log "sudo secure_path now includes /usr/local/bin (${f})"
  else
    echo "WARN: generated sudoers drop-in is invalid, secure_path left as it was" >&2
  fi
  rm -f "$tmp"
}

ci_jobs_running() { docker ps -q --filter "label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true" 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }

require_runner_idle() {
  local n
  n=$(ci_jobs_running)
  (( n == 0 )) || die "${n} CI job container(s) running, retry when the runner is idle"
}

# Timestamped copy before a change. Skipped when an identical backup already exists, so reruns of an idempotent
# installer do not pile up copies of an unchanged file.
backup() {
  local f=$1 b
  for b in "${f}".bak.*; do
    [[ -f "$b" ]] && cmp -s "$f" "$b" && return 0
  done
  cp -a "$f" "${f}.bak.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
}

# Prometheus scrape jobs for the logging stack, shared by runner/install/logging.sh and runner/install/update.sh.
prom_jobs_logging() {
  cat <<'YAML'
  - job_name: loki
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:3100"]
  - job_name: alloy
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:12345"]
YAML
}

# Merge KEY=VALUE entries into the single [[runners]] environment line of config.toml.
runner_env_merge() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml existing list key e line
  [[ $(grep -c '^\[\[runners\]\]' "$cfg") -eq 1 ]] || die "expected exactly one [[runners]] block in $cfg"
  existing=$(grep -E '^  environment = \[' "$cfg" | sed -E 's/^  environment = \[(.*)\]$/\1/' || true)
  list="$existing"
  for e in "$@"; do
    key="${e%%=*}"
    if grep -q "\"${key}=" <<<"$list"; then
      list=$(sed -E "s|\"${key}=[^\"]*\"|\"${e}\"|" <<<"$list")
    else
      list="${list:+${list}, }\"${e}\""
    fi
  done
  line="  environment = [${list}]"
  if grep -qE '^  environment = \[' "$cfg"; then
    sed -i -E "s|^  environment = \[.*\]$|${line}|" "$cfg"
  else
    sed -i -E "0,/^  executor = .*/s||${line}\n&|" "$cfg"
  fi
  chmod 600 "$cfg"
  runner_cfg_owner
}

# When the runner service runs as gitlab-runner (runner/install/users.sh), it must own its config after root edits it.
runner_cfg_owner() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
  if [[ -f /etc/systemd/system/gitlab-runner.service.d/10-service-user.conf ]] && id gitlab-runner >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    chown -R gitlab-runner:gitlab-runner /etc/gitlab-runner
    chmod 600 "$cfg"
  fi
}

# --- service user for the monitoring and logging stacks (created by runner/install/users.sh) ---------------
MON_USER="${MON_USER:-monitoring}"

# Set MON_UID and MON_GID, or stop if the service user is missing.
mon_ids() {
  id "$MON_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "service user ${MON_USER} missing, run runner/install/users.sh first"
  MON_UID=$(id -u "$MON_USER")
  MON_GID=$(id -g "$MON_USER")
}

# env_set FILE KEY VALUE: set KEY=VALUE in a root-only env file.
env_set() {
  local f="$1" k="$2" v="$3"
  touch "$f"
  chmod 600 "$f"
  if grep -q "^${k}=" "$f"; then
    sed -i "s|^${k}=.*|${k}=${v}|" "$f"
  else
    printf '%s=%s\n' "$k" "$v" >> "$f"
  fi
}

# stack_ids DIR [logging]: write the ids the compose file needs into DIR/.env.
stack_ids() {
  local dir="$1" docker_gid journal_gid
  mon_ids
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_UID "$MON_UID"
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_GID "$MON_GID"
  if [[ "${2:-}" == logging ]]; then
    docker_gid=$(getent group docker | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$docker_gid" ]] || die "docker group missing"
    env_set "${dir}/.env" DOCKER_GID "$docker_gid"
    journal_gid=$(getent group systemd-journal | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$journal_gid" ]] || die "systemd-journal group missing"
    # compose rejects equal group_add items, so the journal group is only added when it differs from docker's.
    if [[ "$journal_gid" != "$docker_gid" ]]; then
      printf 'services:\n  alloy:\n    group_add:\n      - "%s"\n' "$journal_gid" > "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    else
      rm -f "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    fi
  fi
}

# Config readable by the service user, writable by root only; .env (secrets) stays root-only.
own_stack_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  chown -R "root:${MON_USER}" "$dir"
  chmod -R u=rwX,g=rX,o= "$dir"
  if [[ -f "${dir}/.env" ]]; then
    chown root:root "${dir}/.env"
    chmod 600 "${dir}/.env"
  fi
}

# Create the stack's containers and volumes without starting them, then hand the volumes to the service user.
chown_volumes() {
  local dir="$1" project v
  mon_ids
  project=$(cd "$dir" && docker compose config --format json | jq -r .name)
  (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start --quiet-pull >/dev/null 2>&1) || (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start >/dev/null)
  for v in $(docker volume ls -q --filter "label=com.docker.compose.project=${project}"); do
    docker run --rm --network none -v "${v}:/v" alpine:3.20 chown -R "${MON_UID}:${MON_GID}" /v
  done
}
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/lib.sh"

install -d "$R/runner/install"
cat > "$R/runner/install/register-runner.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Register a docker-executor runner with a glrt- token and apply the job pickup fix.
set -Eeuo pipefail
# shellcheck source=../../common/lib.sh
source "$(dirname "$0")/../../common/lib.sh"

GITLAB_URL="${GITLAB_URL:-https://gitlab.com}"
DESCRIPTION="${DESCRIPTION:-hetzner-$(uname -n)}"
DEFAULT_IMAGE="${DEFAULT_IMAGE:-alpine:3.20}"
REQUEST_CONCURRENCY="${REQUEST_CONCURRENCY:-2}"
CFG=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml

if [[ "${1:-}" == "-h" || "${1:-}" == "--help" ]]; then
  cat <<USAGE
Usage: $0
Env: GITLAB_URL (default https://gitlab.com), DESCRIPTION (default hetzner-<hostname>),
     DEFAULT_IMAGE (default alpine:3.20), REQUEST_CONCURRENCY (default 2)
Per-job limits (memory 1536m, cpus 1.5, shm 256 MB, services 384m, and a ci-jobs.slice with a process and memory ceiling) are added to config.toml; see runner/install/job-limits.sh.
Tags, untagged jobs, protected and locked are set in the GitLab UI when creating the runner.
The token is read interactively and never stored in shell history.
USAGE
  exit 0
fi
require_root
command -v gitlab-runner >/dev/null || die "gitlab-runner not installed"
systemctl is-active --quiet docker || die "docker is not running"
if [[ -f "$CFG" ]] && grep -q '^\[\[runners\]\]' "$CFG"; then
  die "a runner is already registered in ${CFG}. Remove it first: gitlab-runner unregister --all-runners"
fi

read -rsp "Runner authentication token (glrt-...): " token; echo
[[ "$token" == glrt-* ]] || die "token must start with glrt-"

[[ -f "$CFG" ]] && backup "$CFG"
gitlab-runner register --non-interactive \
  --url "$GITLAB_URL" \
  --token "$token" \
  --executor docker \
  --docker-image "$DEFAULT_IMAGE" \
  --description "$DESCRIPTION" \
  --docker-pull-policy "if-not-present" \
  --docker-volumes "/cache" >/dev/null
unset token

if ! grep -q 'request_concurrency' "$CFG"; then
  sed -i -E "0,/^  name = .*/s//&\n  request_concurrency = ${REQUEST_CONCURRENCY}/" "$CFG"
fi
chmod 600 "$CFG"
# Per-job CPU, memory and process limits (runner/install/job-limits.sh); the runner reloads config.toml by itself.
bash "$(dirname "$0")/job-limits.sh" >/dev/null || log "WARN: job limits not set; run runner/install/job-limits.sh"
runner_cfg_owner
systemctl restart gitlab-runner
sleep 3

gitlab-runner verify 2>&1 | grep -E 'is valid|is not valid' || true
grep -nE '^concurrent|^listen_address|request_concurrency|executor' "$CFG"
if grep -qi bottleneck <<<"$(journalctl -u gitlab-runner --since "-1min" --no-pager)"; then
  log "WARN: runner still reports a request bottleneck"
else
  log "OK: runner registered"
fi
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/runner/install/register-runner.sh"

install -d "$R/runner/install"
cat > "$R/runner/install/job-limits.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Per-job CPU, memory, swap and /dev/shm limits in [runners.docker] of /etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml, plus a systemd
# slice (ci-jobs.slice) that caps the process count and, optionally, the memory of ALL job containers together, so
# one job cannot take the whole host. The limits cover the build container and its `services:` containers (postgres,
# redis, ...). Backs up the file, changes only these keys, and is safe to rerun. A value you already set in
# config.toml is kept unless you pass it in the environment. Swap follows memory (no swap) unless JOB_MEMORY_SWAP
# (JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP) is given or the file already has a memory_swap (service_memory_swap).
#
# GitLab Runner has no per-container process limit, so processes are limited per host through the slice: the jobs
# together can start at most JOBS_TASKS_MAX processes and threads (default 4096), and a fork bomb ends there instead of
# exhausting the host. JOBS_MEMORY_MAX (default 3G) is a ceiling for all job containers together; "" or "infinity" turns it off.
#
# Defaults suit a CAX11 (2 vCPU, 4 GB RAM) with concurrent = 2: two jobs at 1.5 CPU and 1.5 GB, services at 384 MB,
# and a 3 GB ceiling for all of them leave about 1 GB for the host and its stacks. A bigger host: raise JOB_MEMORY,
# JOB_CPUS and JOBS_MEMORY_MAX and keep concurrent x limit below the hardware. A job that needs more fails with an
# out-of-memory kill (exit 137) instead of freezing the host.
# gitlab-runner reloads config.toml by itself; running jobs keep their old limits, new jobs get the new ones.
#
# Env: JOB_MEMORY (default 1536m), JOB_MEMORY_SWAP (default = JOB_MEMORY, i.e. no swap), JOB_CPUS (default 1.5),
#      JOB_SHM_SIZE in bytes (default 268435456), JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY (default 384m), JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP
#      (default = JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY), JOB_SERVICE_CPUS (default 0.5), JOBS_TASKS_MAX (default 4096),
#      JOBS_MEMORY_MAX (default 3G)
set -Eeuo pipefail
# shellcheck source=../../common/lib.sh
source "$(dirname "$0")/../../common/lib.sh"

CFG="${RUNNER_CONFIG:-/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml}"

if [[ "${1:-}" == "-h" || "${1:-}" == "--help" ]]; then
  sed -n '2,22p' "$0" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'
  exit 0
fi
require_root
[[ -f "$CFG" ]] && grep -q '^[[:space:]]*\[runners.docker\]' "$CFG" || die "no [runners.docker] section in ${CFG}: register the runner first (runner/install/register-runner.sh)"

mem_default="${JOB_MEMORY:-1536m}"
[[ "$mem_default" =~ ^[0-9]+[kmg]$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_MEMORY: ${mem_default} (for example 1536m)"
swap_default="${JOB_MEMORY_SWAP:-$mem_default}"
[[ "$swap_default" =~ ^[0-9]+[kmg]$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_MEMORY_SWAP: ${swap_default}"
cpus_default="${JOB_CPUS:-1.5}"
[[ "$cpus_default" =~ ^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_CPUS: ${cpus_default}"
shm_default="${JOB_SHM_SIZE:-268435456}"
[[ "$shm_default" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_SHM_SIZE: ${shm_default}"
smem_default="${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY:-384m}"
[[ "$smem_default" =~ ^[0-9]+[kmg]$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY: ${smem_default}"
sswap_default="${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP:-$smem_default}"
[[ "$sswap_default" =~ ^[0-9]+[kmg]$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP: ${sswap_default}"
scpus_default="${JOB_SERVICE_CPUS:-0.5}"
[[ "$scpus_default" =~ ^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_SERVICE_CPUS: ${scpus_default}"
tasks_max="${JOBS_TASKS_MAX:-4096}"
[[ "$tasks_max" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || die "invalid JOBS_TASKS_MAX: ${tasks_max}"
mem_max="${JOBS_MEMORY_MAX-3G}"
[[ -z "$mem_max" || "$mem_max" == "infinity" || "$mem_max" =~ ^[0-9]+[KMGkmg]?$ ]] || die "invalid JOBS_MEMORY_MAX: ${mem_max} (for example 3G)"
# systemd only accepts upper-case K/M/G suffixes; a lower-case value would be ignored with a warning.
mem_max="${mem_max^^}"
[[ "$mem_max" == "INFINITY" ]] && mem_max="infinity"

# The slice is the parent cgroup of every job and service container (Docker uses the systemd cgroup driver on these hosts).
slice="${JOBS_SLICE_FILE:-/etc/systemd/system/ci-jobs.slice}"
slice_tmp=$(mktemp)
tmp=$(mktemp)
trap 'rm -f "$tmp" "$slice_tmp"' EXIT
{
  echo "# Written by runner/install/job-limits.sh: ceiling for all CI job containers together."
  echo "[Slice]"
  echo "TasksMax=${tasks_max}"
  if [[ -n "$mem_max" && "$mem_max" != "infinity" ]]; then echo "MemoryMax=${mem_max}"; fi
} > "$slice_tmp"
if ! cmp -s "$slice_tmp" "$slice" 2>/dev/null; then
  install -m 0644 "$slice_tmp" "$slice"
  systemctl daemon-reload 2>/dev/null || true
  log "wrote ${slice}"
fi

# Lines: key, value, force (1 when the environment gave the value: it then replaces what config.toml has).
# memory_swap and service_memory_swap follow their memory key when neither is set.
specs=$(printf '%s\t%s\t%s\n' \
  memory "\"${mem_default}\"" "${JOB_MEMORY:+1}" \
  memory_swap "\"${swap_default}\"" "${JOB_MEMORY_SWAP:+1}${JOB_MEMORY:+1}" \
  cpus "\"${cpus_default}\"" "${JOB_CPUS:+1}" \
  shm_size "${shm_default}" "${JOB_SHM_SIZE:+1}" \
  cgroup_parent '"ci-jobs.slice"' 1 \
  service_memory "\"${smem_default}\"" "${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY:+1}" \
  service_memory_swap "\"${sswap_default}\"" "${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP:+1}${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY:+1}" \
  service_cpus "\"${scpus_default}\"" "${JOB_SERVICE_CPUS:+1}" \
  service_cgroup_parent '"ci-jobs.slice"' 1)
awk -v specs="$specs" '
  function flush(   k) {
    if (!insec) return
    # Without its own value, swap follows the memory limit of this section (swap below memory is rejected by Docker).
    if (!seen["memory_swap"] && curmem != "") vals["memory_swap"] = curmem
    if (!seen["service_memory_swap"] && cursmem != "") vals["service_memory_swap"] = cursmem
    for (k = 1; k <= n; k++) if (!seen[names[k]]) print "    " names[k] " = " vals[names[k]]
    insec = 0
  }
  function val(line,   v) { v = line; sub(/^[^=]*=[[:space:]]*/, "", v); sub(/[[:space:]]*#.*$/, "", v); return v }
  BEGIN {
    n = split(specs, lines, "\n")
    for (i = 1; i <= n; i++) { split(lines[i], f, "\t"); names[i] = f[1]; vals[f[1]] = f[2]; force[f[1]] = f[3] }
  }
  /^[[:space:]]*\[/ { flush() }
  /^[[:space:]]*\[runners\.docker\][[:space:]]*$/ { insec = 1; curmem = ""; cursmem = ""; split("", seen); print; next }
  insec {
    # pids_limit is not a GitLab Runner key (an older version of this script wrote it): drop it.
    if ($0 ~ "^[[:space:]]*pids_limit[[:space:]]*=") next
    for (k = 1; k <= n; k++) {
      nm = names[k]
      if ($0 ~ "^[[:space:]]*" nm "[[:space:]]*=") {
        seen[nm] = 1
        if (nm == "memory") curmem = val($0)
        if (nm == "service_memory") cursmem = val($0)
        if (force[nm]) { if (nm == "memory") curmem = vals[nm]; if (nm == "service_memory") cursmem = vals[nm]; print "    " nm " = " vals[nm]; next }
      }
    }
  }
  { print }
  END { flush() }
' "$CFG" > "$tmp"

if cmp -s "$tmp" "$CFG"; then
  log "job limits already set in ${CFG}"
else
  backup "$CFG"
  cat "$tmp" > "$CFG"
  log "job limits written to ${CFG}"
fi
awk '/^[[:space:]]*\[runners\.docker\]/ { s = 1; next } /^[[:space:]]*\[/ { s = 0 } s && /^[[:space:]]*(memory|memory_swap|cpus|shm_size|cgroup_parent|service_[a-z_]+)[[:space:]]*=/' "$CFG"
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/runner/install/job-limits.sh"

echo "files written to $R: 3"
INSTALL
sudo DESCRIPTION=hetzner-cax11 bash /root/server-init-config/runner/install/register-runner.sh
````

<a id="job-limits"></a>
## Per-job limits

Adds memory 1536m (no swap), 1.5 CPUs and 256 MB /dev/shm per job, smaller limits for `services:` containers, and a `ci-jobs.slice` (4096 processes, 3 GB memory for all jobs together) to `[runners.docker]` in `config.toml` (a backup is made, values you set yourself are kept). Needs a registered runner. Change them with `JOB_MEMORY`, `JOB_CPUS`, `JOB_SHM_SIZE`, `JOBS_TASKS_MAX`, `JOBS_MEMORY_MAX` on the last line.

````bash
sudo bash <<'INSTALL'
set -Eeuo pipefail
R=/root/server-init-config

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/lib.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Shared helpers for the installers (runner/install, server and common). Sourced, not executed.
# shellcheck shell=bash

REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
export REPO_ROOT

log() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

require_root() { [[ $EUID -eq 0 ]] || die "run as root"; }

selinux_on() { command -v selinuxenabled >/dev/null 2>&1 && selinuxenabled; }

pkg_install() {
  if command -v dnf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    dnf -y -q install "$@"
  elif command -v apt-get >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    apt-get update -q && apt-get install -y -q "$@"
  else
    die "no supported package manager"
  fi
}

# Label a directory so containers may read files in it (no-op without SELinux).
selinux_label_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  install -d -m 0755 "$dir"
  selinux_on || return 0
  command -v semanage >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_install policycoreutils-python-utils
  # Read the whole list first: grep -q exits at the first match and would SIGPIPE the producer under pipefail.
  if ! grep -q "^${dir}(/\.\*)?" <<<"$(semanage fcontext -l)"; then
    semanage fcontext -a -t container_file_t "${dir}(/.*)?"
  fi
  restorecon -R "$dir"
}

restorecon_if() { if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon "$@" || true; fi; }

# RHEL-family sudo resets PATH to secure_path, which can leave out /usr/local/bin (so `sudo health` fails).
# Adds a validated drop-in unless some sudoers file already sets a secure_path that includes it.
ensure_secure_path() {
  local f=/etc/sudoers.d/50-secure-path tmp
  command -v visudo >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  if grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path[[:space:]]*=.*/usr/local/bin' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0
  fi
  if ! grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0   # no secure_path at all (Debian family keeps it in sudoers; others use the caller PATH)
  fi
  tmp=$(mktemp)
  printf 'Defaults secure_path="/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"\n' > "$tmp"
  if visudo -cqf "$tmp"; then
    install -d -m 0750 /etc/sudoers.d
    install -m 0440 -o root -g root "$tmp" "$f"
    log "sudo secure_path now includes /usr/local/bin (${f})"
  else
    echo "WARN: generated sudoers drop-in is invalid, secure_path left as it was" >&2
  fi
  rm -f "$tmp"
}

ci_jobs_running() { docker ps -q --filter "label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true" 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }

require_runner_idle() {
  local n
  n=$(ci_jobs_running)
  (( n == 0 )) || die "${n} CI job container(s) running, retry when the runner is idle"
}

# Timestamped copy before a change. Skipped when an identical backup already exists, so reruns of an idempotent
# installer do not pile up copies of an unchanged file.
backup() {
  local f=$1 b
  for b in "${f}".bak.*; do
    [[ -f "$b" ]] && cmp -s "$f" "$b" && return 0
  done
  cp -a "$f" "${f}.bak.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
}

# Prometheus scrape jobs for the logging stack, shared by runner/install/logging.sh and runner/install/update.sh.
prom_jobs_logging() {
  cat <<'YAML'
  - job_name: loki
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:3100"]
  - job_name: alloy
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:12345"]
YAML
}

# Merge KEY=VALUE entries into the single [[runners]] environment line of config.toml.
runner_env_merge() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml existing list key e line
  [[ $(grep -c '^\[\[runners\]\]' "$cfg") -eq 1 ]] || die "expected exactly one [[runners]] block in $cfg"
  existing=$(grep -E '^  environment = \[' "$cfg" | sed -E 's/^  environment = \[(.*)\]$/\1/' || true)
  list="$existing"
  for e in "$@"; do
    key="${e%%=*}"
    if grep -q "\"${key}=" <<<"$list"; then
      list=$(sed -E "s|\"${key}=[^\"]*\"|\"${e}\"|" <<<"$list")
    else
      list="${list:+${list}, }\"${e}\""
    fi
  done
  line="  environment = [${list}]"
  if grep -qE '^  environment = \[' "$cfg"; then
    sed -i -E "s|^  environment = \[.*\]$|${line}|" "$cfg"
  else
    sed -i -E "0,/^  executor = .*/s||${line}\n&|" "$cfg"
  fi
  chmod 600 "$cfg"
  runner_cfg_owner
}

# When the runner service runs as gitlab-runner (runner/install/users.sh), it must own its config after root edits it.
runner_cfg_owner() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
  if [[ -f /etc/systemd/system/gitlab-runner.service.d/10-service-user.conf ]] && id gitlab-runner >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    chown -R gitlab-runner:gitlab-runner /etc/gitlab-runner
    chmod 600 "$cfg"
  fi
}

# --- service user for the monitoring and logging stacks (created by runner/install/users.sh) ---------------
MON_USER="${MON_USER:-monitoring}"

# Set MON_UID and MON_GID, or stop if the service user is missing.
mon_ids() {
  id "$MON_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "service user ${MON_USER} missing, run runner/install/users.sh first"
  MON_UID=$(id -u "$MON_USER")
  MON_GID=$(id -g "$MON_USER")
}

# env_set FILE KEY VALUE: set KEY=VALUE in a root-only env file.
env_set() {
  local f="$1" k="$2" v="$3"
  touch "$f"
  chmod 600 "$f"
  if grep -q "^${k}=" "$f"; then
    sed -i "s|^${k}=.*|${k}=${v}|" "$f"
  else
    printf '%s=%s\n' "$k" "$v" >> "$f"
  fi
}

# stack_ids DIR [logging]: write the ids the compose file needs into DIR/.env.
stack_ids() {
  local dir="$1" docker_gid journal_gid
  mon_ids
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_UID "$MON_UID"
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_GID "$MON_GID"
  if [[ "${2:-}" == logging ]]; then
    docker_gid=$(getent group docker | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$docker_gid" ]] || die "docker group missing"
    env_set "${dir}/.env" DOCKER_GID "$docker_gid"
    journal_gid=$(getent group systemd-journal | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$journal_gid" ]] || die "systemd-journal group missing"
    # compose rejects equal group_add items, so the journal group is only added when it differs from docker's.
    if [[ "$journal_gid" != "$docker_gid" ]]; then
      printf 'services:\n  alloy:\n    group_add:\n      - "%s"\n' "$journal_gid" > "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    else
      rm -f "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    fi
  fi
}

# Config readable by the service user, writable by root only; .env (secrets) stays root-only.
own_stack_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  chown -R "root:${MON_USER}" "$dir"
  chmod -R u=rwX,g=rX,o= "$dir"
  if [[ -f "${dir}/.env" ]]; then
    chown root:root "${dir}/.env"
    chmod 600 "${dir}/.env"
  fi
}

# Create the stack's containers and volumes without starting them, then hand the volumes to the service user.
chown_volumes() {
  local dir="$1" project v
  mon_ids
  project=$(cd "$dir" && docker compose config --format json | jq -r .name)
  (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start --quiet-pull >/dev/null 2>&1) || (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start >/dev/null)
  for v in $(docker volume ls -q --filter "label=com.docker.compose.project=${project}"); do
    docker run --rm --network none -v "${v}:/v" alpine:3.20 chown -R "${MON_UID}:${MON_GID}" /v
  done
}
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/lib.sh"

install -d "$R/runner/install"
cat > "$R/runner/install/job-limits.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Per-job CPU, memory, swap and /dev/shm limits in [runners.docker] of /etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml, plus a systemd
# slice (ci-jobs.slice) that caps the process count and, optionally, the memory of ALL job containers together, so
# one job cannot take the whole host. The limits cover the build container and its `services:` containers (postgres,
# redis, ...). Backs up the file, changes only these keys, and is safe to rerun. A value you already set in
# config.toml is kept unless you pass it in the environment. Swap follows memory (no swap) unless JOB_MEMORY_SWAP
# (JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP) is given or the file already has a memory_swap (service_memory_swap).
#
# GitLab Runner has no per-container process limit, so processes are limited per host through the slice: the jobs
# together can start at most JOBS_TASKS_MAX processes and threads (default 4096), and a fork bomb ends there instead of
# exhausting the host. JOBS_MEMORY_MAX (default 3G) is a ceiling for all job containers together; "" or "infinity" turns it off.
#
# Defaults suit a CAX11 (2 vCPU, 4 GB RAM) with concurrent = 2: two jobs at 1.5 CPU and 1.5 GB, services at 384 MB,
# and a 3 GB ceiling for all of them leave about 1 GB for the host and its stacks. A bigger host: raise JOB_MEMORY,
# JOB_CPUS and JOBS_MEMORY_MAX and keep concurrent x limit below the hardware. A job that needs more fails with an
# out-of-memory kill (exit 137) instead of freezing the host.
# gitlab-runner reloads config.toml by itself; running jobs keep their old limits, new jobs get the new ones.
#
# Env: JOB_MEMORY (default 1536m), JOB_MEMORY_SWAP (default = JOB_MEMORY, i.e. no swap), JOB_CPUS (default 1.5),
#      JOB_SHM_SIZE in bytes (default 268435456), JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY (default 384m), JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP
#      (default = JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY), JOB_SERVICE_CPUS (default 0.5), JOBS_TASKS_MAX (default 4096),
#      JOBS_MEMORY_MAX (default 3G)
set -Eeuo pipefail
# shellcheck source=../../common/lib.sh
source "$(dirname "$0")/../../common/lib.sh"

CFG="${RUNNER_CONFIG:-/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml}"

if [[ "${1:-}" == "-h" || "${1:-}" == "--help" ]]; then
  sed -n '2,22p' "$0" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'
  exit 0
fi
require_root
[[ -f "$CFG" ]] && grep -q '^[[:space:]]*\[runners.docker\]' "$CFG" || die "no [runners.docker] section in ${CFG}: register the runner first (runner/install/register-runner.sh)"

mem_default="${JOB_MEMORY:-1536m}"
[[ "$mem_default" =~ ^[0-9]+[kmg]$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_MEMORY: ${mem_default} (for example 1536m)"
swap_default="${JOB_MEMORY_SWAP:-$mem_default}"
[[ "$swap_default" =~ ^[0-9]+[kmg]$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_MEMORY_SWAP: ${swap_default}"
cpus_default="${JOB_CPUS:-1.5}"
[[ "$cpus_default" =~ ^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_CPUS: ${cpus_default}"
shm_default="${JOB_SHM_SIZE:-268435456}"
[[ "$shm_default" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_SHM_SIZE: ${shm_default}"
smem_default="${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY:-384m}"
[[ "$smem_default" =~ ^[0-9]+[kmg]$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY: ${smem_default}"
sswap_default="${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP:-$smem_default}"
[[ "$sswap_default" =~ ^[0-9]+[kmg]$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP: ${sswap_default}"
scpus_default="${JOB_SERVICE_CPUS:-0.5}"
[[ "$scpus_default" =~ ^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$ ]] || die "invalid JOB_SERVICE_CPUS: ${scpus_default}"
tasks_max="${JOBS_TASKS_MAX:-4096}"
[[ "$tasks_max" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || die "invalid JOBS_TASKS_MAX: ${tasks_max}"
mem_max="${JOBS_MEMORY_MAX-3G}"
[[ -z "$mem_max" || "$mem_max" == "infinity" || "$mem_max" =~ ^[0-9]+[KMGkmg]?$ ]] || die "invalid JOBS_MEMORY_MAX: ${mem_max} (for example 3G)"
# systemd only accepts upper-case K/M/G suffixes; a lower-case value would be ignored with a warning.
mem_max="${mem_max^^}"
[[ "$mem_max" == "INFINITY" ]] && mem_max="infinity"

# The slice is the parent cgroup of every job and service container (Docker uses the systemd cgroup driver on these hosts).
slice="${JOBS_SLICE_FILE:-/etc/systemd/system/ci-jobs.slice}"
slice_tmp=$(mktemp)
tmp=$(mktemp)
trap 'rm -f "$tmp" "$slice_tmp"' EXIT
{
  echo "# Written by runner/install/job-limits.sh: ceiling for all CI job containers together."
  echo "[Slice]"
  echo "TasksMax=${tasks_max}"
  if [[ -n "$mem_max" && "$mem_max" != "infinity" ]]; then echo "MemoryMax=${mem_max}"; fi
} > "$slice_tmp"
if ! cmp -s "$slice_tmp" "$slice" 2>/dev/null; then
  install -m 0644 "$slice_tmp" "$slice"
  systemctl daemon-reload 2>/dev/null || true
  log "wrote ${slice}"
fi

# Lines: key, value, force (1 when the environment gave the value: it then replaces what config.toml has).
# memory_swap and service_memory_swap follow their memory key when neither is set.
specs=$(printf '%s\t%s\t%s\n' \
  memory "\"${mem_default}\"" "${JOB_MEMORY:+1}" \
  memory_swap "\"${swap_default}\"" "${JOB_MEMORY_SWAP:+1}${JOB_MEMORY:+1}" \
  cpus "\"${cpus_default}\"" "${JOB_CPUS:+1}" \
  shm_size "${shm_default}" "${JOB_SHM_SIZE:+1}" \
  cgroup_parent '"ci-jobs.slice"' 1 \
  service_memory "\"${smem_default}\"" "${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY:+1}" \
  service_memory_swap "\"${sswap_default}\"" "${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY_SWAP:+1}${JOB_SERVICE_MEMORY:+1}" \
  service_cpus "\"${scpus_default}\"" "${JOB_SERVICE_CPUS:+1}" \
  service_cgroup_parent '"ci-jobs.slice"' 1)
awk -v specs="$specs" '
  function flush(   k) {
    if (!insec) return
    # Without its own value, swap follows the memory limit of this section (swap below memory is rejected by Docker).
    if (!seen["memory_swap"] && curmem != "") vals["memory_swap"] = curmem
    if (!seen["service_memory_swap"] && cursmem != "") vals["service_memory_swap"] = cursmem
    for (k = 1; k <= n; k++) if (!seen[names[k]]) print "    " names[k] " = " vals[names[k]]
    insec = 0
  }
  function val(line,   v) { v = line; sub(/^[^=]*=[[:space:]]*/, "", v); sub(/[[:space:]]*#.*$/, "", v); return v }
  BEGIN {
    n = split(specs, lines, "\n")
    for (i = 1; i <= n; i++) { split(lines[i], f, "\t"); names[i] = f[1]; vals[f[1]] = f[2]; force[f[1]] = f[3] }
  }
  /^[[:space:]]*\[/ { flush() }
  /^[[:space:]]*\[runners\.docker\][[:space:]]*$/ { insec = 1; curmem = ""; cursmem = ""; split("", seen); print; next }
  insec {
    # pids_limit is not a GitLab Runner key (an older version of this script wrote it): drop it.
    if ($0 ~ "^[[:space:]]*pids_limit[[:space:]]*=") next
    for (k = 1; k <= n; k++) {
      nm = names[k]
      if ($0 ~ "^[[:space:]]*" nm "[[:space:]]*=") {
        seen[nm] = 1
        if (nm == "memory") curmem = val($0)
        if (nm == "service_memory") cursmem = val($0)
        if (force[nm]) { if (nm == "memory") curmem = vals[nm]; if (nm == "service_memory") cursmem = vals[nm]; print "    " nm " = " vals[nm]; next }
      }
    }
  }
  { print }
  END { flush() }
' "$CFG" > "$tmp"

if cmp -s "$tmp" "$CFG"; then
  log "job limits already set in ${CFG}"
else
  backup "$CFG"
  cat "$tmp" > "$CFG"
  log "job limits written to ${CFG}"
fi
awk '/^[[:space:]]*\[runners\.docker\]/ { s = 1; next } /^[[:space:]]*\[/ { s = 0 } s && /^[[:space:]]*(memory|memory_swap|cpus|shm_size|cgroup_parent|service_[a-z_]+)[[:space:]]*=/' "$CFG"
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/runner/install/job-limits.sh"

echo "files written to $R: 2"
bash "$R/runner/install/job-limits.sh" </dev/null
INSTALL
````

<a id="registry-cache"></a>
## Docker Hub pull-through cache

Cache on `127.0.0.1:5000`, set as the Docker mirror. Restarts Docker: run it while no jobs are running.

````bash
sudo bash <<'INSTALL'
set -Eeuo pipefail
R=/root/server-init-config

install -d "$R/common"
cat > "$R/common/lib.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Shared helpers for the installers (runner/install, server and common). Sourced, not executed.
# shellcheck shell=bash

REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
export REPO_ROOT

log() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

require_root() { [[ $EUID -eq 0 ]] || die "run as root"; }

selinux_on() { command -v selinuxenabled >/dev/null 2>&1 && selinuxenabled; }

pkg_install() {
  if command -v dnf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    dnf -y -q install "$@"
  elif command -v apt-get >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    apt-get update -q && apt-get install -y -q "$@"
  else
    die "no supported package manager"
  fi
}

# Label a directory so containers may read files in it (no-op without SELinux).
selinux_label_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  install -d -m 0755 "$dir"
  selinux_on || return 0
  command -v semanage >/dev/null 2>&1 || pkg_install policycoreutils-python-utils
  # Read the whole list first: grep -q exits at the first match and would SIGPIPE the producer under pipefail.
  if ! grep -q "^${dir}(/\.\*)?" <<<"$(semanage fcontext -l)"; then
    semanage fcontext -a -t container_file_t "${dir}(/.*)?"
  fi
  restorecon -R "$dir"
}

restorecon_if() { if command -v restorecon >/dev/null 2>&1; then restorecon "$@" || true; fi; }

# RHEL-family sudo resets PATH to secure_path, which can leave out /usr/local/bin (so `sudo health` fails).
# Adds a validated drop-in unless some sudoers file already sets a secure_path that includes it.
ensure_secure_path() {
  local f=/etc/sudoers.d/50-secure-path tmp
  command -v visudo >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  if grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path[[:space:]]*=.*/usr/local/bin' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0
  fi
  if ! grep -rhsE '^[[:space:]]*Defaults[[:space:]]+secure_path' /etc/sudoers /etc/sudoers.d 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
    return 0   # no secure_path at all (Debian family keeps it in sudoers; others use the caller PATH)
  fi
  tmp=$(mktemp)
  printf 'Defaults secure_path="/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"\n' > "$tmp"
  if visudo -cqf "$tmp"; then
    install -d -m 0750 /etc/sudoers.d
    install -m 0440 -o root -g root "$tmp" "$f"
    log "sudo secure_path now includes /usr/local/bin (${f})"
  else
    echo "WARN: generated sudoers drop-in is invalid, secure_path left as it was" >&2
  fi
  rm -f "$tmp"
}

ci_jobs_running() { docker ps -q --filter "label=com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true" 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }

require_runner_idle() {
  local n
  n=$(ci_jobs_running)
  (( n == 0 )) || die "${n} CI job container(s) running, retry when the runner is idle"
}

# Timestamped copy before a change. Skipped when an identical backup already exists, so reruns of an idempotent
# installer do not pile up copies of an unchanged file.
backup() {
  local f=$1 b
  for b in "${f}".bak.*; do
    [[ -f "$b" ]] && cmp -s "$f" "$b" && return 0
  done
  cp -a "$f" "${f}.bak.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
}

# Prometheus scrape jobs for the logging stack, shared by runner/install/logging.sh and runner/install/update.sh.
prom_jobs_logging() {
  cat <<'YAML'
  - job_name: loki
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:3100"]
  - job_name: alloy
    static_configs:
      - targets: ["127.0.0.1:12345"]
YAML
}

# Merge KEY=VALUE entries into the single [[runners]] environment line of config.toml.
runner_env_merge() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml existing list key e line
  [[ $(grep -c '^\[\[runners\]\]' "$cfg") -eq 1 ]] || die "expected exactly one [[runners]] block in $cfg"
  existing=$(grep -E '^  environment = \[' "$cfg" | sed -E 's/^  environment = \[(.*)\]$/\1/' || true)
  list="$existing"
  for e in "$@"; do
    key="${e%%=*}"
    if grep -q "\"${key}=" <<<"$list"; then
      list=$(sed -E "s|\"${key}=[^\"]*\"|\"${e}\"|" <<<"$list")
    else
      list="${list:+${list}, }\"${e}\""
    fi
  done
  line="  environment = [${list}]"
  if grep -qE '^  environment = \[' "$cfg"; then
    sed -i -E "s|^  environment = \[.*\]$|${line}|" "$cfg"
  else
    sed -i -E "0,/^  executor = .*/s||${line}\n&|" "$cfg"
  fi
  chmod 600 "$cfg"
  runner_cfg_owner
}

# When the runner service runs as gitlab-runner (runner/install/users.sh), it must own its config after root edits it.
runner_cfg_owner() {
  local cfg=/etc/gitlab-runner/config.toml
  if [[ -f /etc/systemd/system/gitlab-runner.service.d/10-service-user.conf ]] && id gitlab-runner >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    chown -R gitlab-runner:gitlab-runner /etc/gitlab-runner
    chmod 600 "$cfg"
  fi
}

# --- service user for the monitoring and logging stacks (created by runner/install/users.sh) ---------------
MON_USER="${MON_USER:-monitoring}"

# Set MON_UID and MON_GID, or stop if the service user is missing.
mon_ids() {
  id "$MON_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "service user ${MON_USER} missing, run runner/install/users.sh first"
  MON_UID=$(id -u "$MON_USER")
  MON_GID=$(id -g "$MON_USER")
}

# env_set FILE KEY VALUE: set KEY=VALUE in a root-only env file.
env_set() {
  local f="$1" k="$2" v="$3"
  touch "$f"
  chmod 600 "$f"
  if grep -q "^${k}=" "$f"; then
    sed -i "s|^${k}=.*|${k}=${v}|" "$f"
  else
    printf '%s=%s\n' "$k" "$v" >> "$f"
  fi
}

# stack_ids DIR [logging]: write the ids the compose file needs into DIR/.env.
stack_ids() {
  local dir="$1" docker_gid journal_gid
  mon_ids
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_UID "$MON_UID"
  env_set "${dir}/.env" MON_GID "$MON_GID"
  if [[ "${2:-}" == logging ]]; then
    docker_gid=$(getent group docker | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$docker_gid" ]] || die "docker group missing"
    env_set "${dir}/.env" DOCKER_GID "$docker_gid"
    journal_gid=$(getent group systemd-journal | cut -d: -f3)
    [[ -n "$journal_gid" ]] || die "systemd-journal group missing"
    # compose rejects equal group_add items, so the journal group is only added when it differs from docker's.
    if [[ "$journal_gid" != "$docker_gid" ]]; then
      printf 'services:\n  alloy:\n    group_add:\n      - "%s"\n' "$journal_gid" > "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    else
      rm -f "${dir}/docker-compose.override.yml"
    fi
  fi
}

# Config readable by the service user, writable by root only; .env (secrets) stays root-only.
own_stack_dir() {
  local dir="$1"
  chown -R "root:${MON_USER}" "$dir"
  chmod -R u=rwX,g=rX,o= "$dir"
  if [[ -f "${dir}/.env" ]]; then
    chown root:root "${dir}/.env"
    chmod 600 "${dir}/.env"
  fi
}

# Create the stack's containers and volumes without starting them, then hand the volumes to the service user.
chown_volumes() {
  local dir="$1" project v
  mon_ids
  project=$(cd "$dir" && docker compose config --format json | jq -r .name)
  (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start --quiet-pull >/dev/null 2>&1) || (cd "$dir" && docker compose up --no-start >/dev/null)
  for v in $(docker volume ls -q --filter "label=com.docker.compose.project=${project}"); do
    docker run --rm --network none -v "${v}:/v" alpine:3.20 chown -R "${MON_UID}:${MON_GID}" /v
  done
}
__SIC_EOF__
chmod 755 "$R/common/lib.sh"

install -d "$R/runner/install"
cat > "$R/runner/install/registry-cache.sh" <<'__SIC_EOF__'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Docker Hub pull-through cache on 127.0.0.1:5000, wired into the Docker daemon as a mirror.
# Restarts Docker: refuses to run while CI jobs are running.
set -Eeuo pipefail
# shellcheck source=../../common/lib.sh
source "$(dirname "$0")/../../common/lib.sh"

DIR=/opt/registry-cache
MIRROR="http://127.0.0.1:5000"
DAEMON=/etc/docker/daemon.json
PROM_CFG=/opt/monitoring/prometheus/prometheus.yml

if [[ "${1:-}" == "-h" || "${1:-}" == "--help" ]]; then
  echo "Usage: $0  installs the Docker Hub pull-through cache into $DIR"
  exit 0
fi
require_root
command -v jq >/dev/null || pkg_install jq
require_runner_idle
if [[ -f "$DIR/docker-compose.yml" ]]; then
  log "${DIR} exists: re-applying the repo files and finishing the setup (safe to rerun)"
elif ss -ltn | awk '$4 ~ /:(5000|5001)$/' | grep -q .; then
  die "port 5000 or 5001 already in use"
fi

selinux_label_dir "$DIR"
cp -a "$REPO_ROOT/runner/stacks/registry-cache/config.yml" "$REPO_ROOT/runner/stacks/registry-cache/docker-compose.yml" "$DIR/"
restorecon_if -R "$DIR"

cd "$DIR"
docker compose config -q
docker compose pull -q
docker compose up -d
for _ in $(seq 1 15); do curl -fsS "$MIRROR/v2/" >/dev/null 2>&1 && break; sleep 2; done
curl -fsS "$MIRROR/v2/" >/dev/null || { docker compose logs --tail 30; die "registry cache did not start"; }

if ! jq -e --arg m "$MIRROR" '(.["registry-mirrors"] // []) | index($m)' "$DAEMON" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  backup "$DAEMON"
  tmp=$(mktemp)
  jq --arg m "$MIRROR" '.["registry-mirrors"] = [$m]' "$DAEMON" > "$tmp"
  dockerd --validate --config-file "$tmp" >/dev/null || { rm -f "$tmp"; die "new daemon.json is invalid"; }
  install -m 0644 "$tmp" "$DAEMON"
  rm -f "$tmp"
  restorecon_if "$DAEMON"
  log "restarting docker"
````
